## Session: 2026-09-13 00:40
- **Branch:** main
- **Files changed:** DEVLOG.md, frontend/src/api/inventory.js
- **New files:** none
- **Notes:** Made a dead backend diagnose itself. Previously any failure collapsed to "Request failed (500)", which points you at the frontend when the real problem is that the API process is gone. Probed the actual failure mode first: with :4000 down, the Vite proxy answers with a bare text/plain 500 and an empty body, while every genuine Express error returns JSON with a message. That makes an unparseable body a reliable signal the request never reached Express, so the client now shows "Cannot reach the API on localhost:4000..." for that case and leaves real API messages untouched. Verified all three paths: backend down (browser), genuine 404 (keeps "SKU not found"), and Retry recovering once the backend is back.

---

## Session: 2026-09-13 00:50

- **Notes:** Three UI changes. (1) Removed justifyContent:space-between from the hero row so the Baseline/Now chart sits tight against the number it explains instead of being pinned ~700px away at the card edge. (2) Grouped the seven KPIs into two labelled rows along the project's own two competing objectives (service/availability vs working capital), sharing one 4-column grid so cards align between rows. (3) Rebuilt the hint tooltip as theme-aware liquid glass: it was one light panel with dark text in both themes, measuring 4.04:1 over a dark backdrop (under AA), which is why it could not be pushed glassier. Light tint/dark text in Light and dark tint/light text in Dark now measures 7.8-17.9:1 and 5.9-13.9:1 while running a lower fill alpha, plus gradient fill, specular top edge, inner base shade and a prefers-reduced-transparency fallback.

## Session: 2026-09-13 02:20
- **Branch:** main
- **Files changed:** .kiro/specs/mvp1-inventory-visibility/tasks.md, DEVLOG.md, README.md, backend/src/db/seed.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/App.jsx, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, frontend/src/components/Sidebar.jsx
- **New files:** backend/src/db/audit.js, frontend/src/pages/Activity.jsx
- **Notes:** TASK-31 and TASK-32, chosen after auditing what actually stands between the repo and a submission. Two gaps mapped straight onto scored criteria. First: audit_log had been in the schema from day one with a comment naming the events it should hold, and nothing ever wrote to it, while Observability is an explicit judging criterion. Now six real write points, a field-level before/after diff on policy edits, the system proposal stored next to the manager's action on decisions, GET /api/audit, and a new Activity page rendering it as plain-English sentences with the raw payload one click away. ALERT_TRIGGERED deliberately fires inside the dedupe_key branch so it means "condition first became true", not "page was loaded", and seed.js now wipes audit_log with alerts_log or the trail would stay empty forever after a reseed. Second: the README still described the pre-rebuild app (categories, out of stock, top-selling products, in-memory store), which is what a judge reads first. Rewritten around the reasoning loop with a diagram, the domain-rules table, the real API, and an honest statement that TASK-11 is blocked on a key rather than on design. Three defects caught in browser verification, including ColHint rendering only its icon so the page shipped with no visible h1.

- **Notes:** TASK-43. Hardened the prompt, then added the half that does not rely on the model cooperating: a deterministic verifier that checks every figure in the prose against the figures supplied. Pulled llama3.1:8b and qwen3:8b to compare. The first benchmark reported qwen3 at 7/7 clean and it was completely wrong: qwen3 is a reasoning model, spent its capped token budget on internal monologue, and returned nothing at all, and a verifier that only looks for bad things finds none in an empty string. Fixed by sending think:false (which also made it 36% faster) and by failing responses under 80 chars. Also nearly shipped a 1% numeric tolerance that passed 26000 for 26217. With 28 runs per model, llama3 that Stan already had wins on both accuracy and speed and is the only one with zero dangerous drift, so the two downloads produced evidence rather than a new model. Mid-edit I also destroyed explain.js by slicing between two string indices and deleting buildFacts and cacheKey with it; recovered from HEAD and redid it with anchored insert-only replacements.

Implemented the retry policy (dangerous drift is retried then rejected, hedging is accepted immediately: 21 hedges in one run all had correct figures, so retrying them would spend a paid call to delete the word "nearly"). Split the benchmark's score into exact, cosmetic and DANGEROUS, which immediately revealed that the facts fix HAD worked: llama3.1's dangerous drift fell 6 to 2 and stayed there, hidden until then behind a single blended percentage that rated inventing a figure and writing "nearly" as the same event. Then Stan asked for "9 months and 8 days" instead of decimals, which was also a latent bug since my new line said 9.1 while the alert said 9.3. Fixed by computing the string once in the engine. Three bugs found on the way: Number(null) rendering a null cover as "0 days", a circular require that warned instead of throwing, and a grammar leak. Honest result: the readable format made year conversions worse, because "9 months and 8 days" invites "nearly a year" in a way "278 days" did not, so the rule of supplying information instead of forbidding behaviour does not generalise. Kept it anyway, readability was the point and the reject-and-fall-back path means no wrong number reaches the screen.

## Session: 2026-09-13 18:05
- **Branch:** main
- **Files changed:** .kiro/specs/mvp1-inventory-visibility/tasks.md, DEVLOG.md, backend/src/llm/explain.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/pages/Alerts.jsx
- **New files:** backend/src/llm/slots.js
- **Notes:** TASK-45, and it came from Stan's question rather than from me: if the maths is hard coded, why is the model touching numbers at all. He was right that it should not be. The engines always computed the figures, but the model retyped them, and every numeric failure measured today happened in the retyping. Now it writes prose with named placeholders and is rejected if it emits any digit, so a wrong figure is unrepresentable rather than merely detectable. The interesting consequence is that constraining the arithmetic relocated the risk to the argument: on the IDLE alert the model wrote "place an order" with perfectly correct numbers, which is the opposite of the right action and reads as authoritative because the figures check out. Fixed with a digit free semantic brief per alert type. Measured 21 explanations end to end: 14 slots, 7 free text, 0 rejected, 0 dangerous. Honest caveat recorded: on an 8B model slot mode costs real prose quality, and should suit the Bedrock tier much better.

- **Notes:** TASK-52. Stan reported the settings popover as far too transparent. It was not a blur value problem: backdrop-filter makes an element a backdrop root, the popover was nested inside the bar's own .glass-surface, and so its backdrop-filter sampled an empty root and silently did nothing. Only the gradient was rendering. HoverHint portals to document.body, which is the only reason the hint panel he was comparing against frosts correctly. Portaled the popover the same way, positioned fixed against the trigger rect with scroll and resize listeners, extended the outside-click test to cover the portaled panel, and set blur to --hint-blur so it matches the values he tuned rather than approximating them. Added to the CLAUDE.md quirks list because it presents as a styling bug and is a DOM one.

The audit behind it found the actual problem: index.css already declared a seven step scale with a comment calling 12px the absolute floor, and 177 inline fontSize numbers bypassed it, 30 of them below that floor, down to a 9px badge. Stan picked the large print preset. Applied it, but at six steps rather than seven: --text-md sat 1px from --text-base and named the same job ("emphasis"), which is font-weight's work, and the usage counts confirmed nobody could apply it (--text-md twice in the codebase, --text-lg never). Its two usages moved to --text-base, and Dashboard card titles moved to --text-lg so that step finally has a purpose. The tuner's own "uneven ratios" warning was wrong and was rewritten: it demanded a constant ratio, which Tailwind and Material would both fail, and which pushes the body size around to satisfy a number. It now tests the two things that actually go wrong, a pair too close to tell apart and ratios that dip rather than climb. Migrating the 177 inline sizes onto the tokens is still outstanding.

It also says the roughly 150 existing inline literals make "match the surrounding code" the wrong instinct here. Serving the glass tuner locally immediately exposed a bug the artifact wrapper had been hiding: a literal degree sign rendered as mojibake without a charset meta, and the code comment beside it claimed to be using an escape while using the literal. Needs Attention moves to row two at full width, on the grounds that it is the only section on the page carrying actions rather than analysis, and that at half width its Action column was wrapping to three lines on a 1546px display. Cover vs Lead + Safety and Inventory Health pair below it, then Value x Movement full width, which the matrix needed more than the pairing did. Stan asked about collapsing Needs Attention by default with a count as the lure; argued against it and he agreed: a count is strictly less information than the rows it hides, and dense-tool operators resent a dashboard that hides the answer behind a click. Built the partial reveal instead, five rows always visible with a "Show N more" button that names what is behind it, and moved the notification count into the section header as a badge tinted by the worst severity present. The badge stays visible when the section is collapsed, which is the case that would otherwise be silent. The reveal state is not persisted, unlike the collapse state: expanding to see two more rows is momentary, and restoring it next visit would undo the short page it exists to protect.

## Session: 2026-09-14 02:10

- **Notes:** TASK-58. Three refinements Stan asked for on the restructured Dashboard. Key Metrics now carries a visible heading, matching the Section title treatment so it reads as a peer of the other cards; I had argued it did not need one, being the first thing on the page, but a name findable only in a source comment is not a shared name. The partial reveal drops to three rows read in full plus a fourth rendered under a gradient fade, which is the honest version of a scroll cue: a hard cut at row three implies the list ends there. The hidden count is measured against the three FULL rows, not the four rendered, so the teased row is not counted as read. The reveal button is now large and centred under the fade rather than a small link tucked left. First version put the fade and the button in one relative container, so bottom: 0 sat below the button and the fade washed out the control it exists to advertise; the fade is anchored to a wrapper around the table alone now. Finally, setting a filter scrolls Needs Attention back into view, which was the outstanding cost of moving the table above the charts that filter it. Only on set, never on clear, since clearing happens at the table's own chip where the user is already looking. Honours prefers-reduced-motion.

- **Notes:** TASK-60. Bulk edit: export every SKU to CSV, fix it in a spreadsheet, upload it back. GET /api/skus/export emits the editable columns plus four computed context columns that are ignored on the way back in; POST /api/skus/import takes { csv, apply } and returns the same result shape either way, so the preview is literally the apply path with the write skipped rather than a second implementation that could disagree with it. sku_id is the key and is never updated: import cannot create or delete SKUs, because a typo in a key column should not silently remove a product line. Validation runs over the whole file before any write and the writes run in one transaction, so a file with a single bad row changes nothing; tested with a negative number, an unknown SKU and a duplicate key, all three reported with line numbers and applied: false despite apply: true. CSV is hand rolled in db/csv.js rather than adding a dependency, with RFC 4180 quoting, because splitting on commas turns a supplier called "Supplier ABC, Thailand" into a row where every later column lands in the wrong field, silently. Numeric comparison uses a 1e-9 tolerance, without which a spreadsheet round trip reports untouched rows as changed. One "Bulk edit" button in the Inventory header, outlined not filled, opening a two item menu; Add SKU stays the only primary action there. The import is three steps on purpose, choose then review then apply. Modal and ModalBtn were extracted from Inventory.jsx into components/Modal.jsx so the review dialog reuses the shell rather than growing a second one. Imports write one SKU_UPDATED audit event each, tagged source: csv_import, so a bulk change is not a blind spot in the Activity tab. Reseeded after testing.

- **Notes:** TASK-65. The hero chart Stan asked for, built on data that actually exists. GET /api/dashboard/history returns monthly consumption at cost from sales_transactions, with a partial flag per month and an average computed over complete months only. The chart toggles Baseline (the existing two-bar comparison) against 6 months (bars plus a dashed average line). What it deliberately is NOT is a history of Total Inventory Value: nothing records what stock was worth on a past date, and reconstructing it backwards from sales would have to assume no goods were ever received, drawing a smooth line rising into the past that is completely plausible and completely wrong. Twelve months is not offered because the data only spans 2026-03-17 to 2026-09-11. The partial flag is the load-bearing part: March opens mid-month and September is still running, so each holds about half a month of sales, and drawn as plain bars beside a full month they read as demand collapsing, a false story told with true numbers. They are hatched instead, the same encoding StockPositionBar uses for idle stock, and the average line excludes them. The server decides partial rather than the chart, because only the server knows the real coverage window. History is fetched on first switch rather than on mount, since most visits never open it. Separately, confirmed the seed IS deterministic, two consecutive runs both produced totalInventoryValue 3711450; an earlier 3.91M reading during this session came from mutated state, not from the seed, and reseeding restores 3.71M.

- **Notes:** TASK-69. Named the fictional SME and drew it a mark. Puenzhi decomposes in Dzongkha into pun (sibling, kin) and zhi (four), so it means the four siblings rather than the four friends, and that distinction drove the Chinese choice: peng you is too weak a register. Recommended 四海米行, Sihai Mihang, Four Seas Rice Trading. 四海 comes from the Analects, 四海之内皆兄弟也, within the four seas all men are brothers, so two characters carry both halves of Puenzhi at once, the number and the kinship; it is also the register an overseas-Chinese trading house actually uses and it implies importing, which this business does. Alternatives presented were 四友 (literal but boutique) and 四合 (warm but reads invented). The suffix 米行 was chosen over 米業 and 貿易 as what a family rice business in Singapore actually calls itself. Logo sheet covers a primary lockup, a 2x2 seal, an ink version, a reversed version, scale from 16 to 64px, and an in-sidebar preview. The mark carries 米 alone because one character survives 16px and four do not. Typeface is Ma Shan Zheng under the OFL, subset from 5.86 MB to 3.7 KB and inlined, so the logo has no CDN dependency. brand.html is generated: the build script also escapes every non-ASCII character as an HTML entity, which is the fix for the mojibake bug that has now hit three of these pages. A 2x2 CSS grid bug was caught in the browser, declaring only columns with auto-flow column gave one row of four and the seal read backwards. Not yet integrated into the app; the sheet argues for the rice company as the TENANT beside StockSense rather than replacing its branding, since swapping the mark would claim the tool belongs to the customer.

- **Notes:** TASK-76. Stan picked option C and asked for a PowerPoint-style drag and drop editor so he can rearrange it himself and then argue with me about the result. Built, with one deliberate constraint: it snaps to ZONES AND ORDER, never to pixels. Free positioning would let a layout be designed here that cannot be built responsively, so the editor offers exactly what a CSS grid with flex columns can express, which means anything arranged in it can actually ship. Three zones, full width plus two columns, nine draggable blocks (seal, wordmark, question, sub-line, the three workspaces, the StockSense credit and the team credit), sliders for column split, gaps, top and side space, seal and wordmark size, and toggles for tiles versus rows and centred text. Insertion point is computed against the midpoint of each existing block so the gap opens where the cursor is rather than always appending. The export is a HOME_LAYOUT object I can apply directly. It also argues back live: a critique panel under the canvas objects as he arranges, catching the workspaces being split across zones, the sub-line ordered before the question it explains, StockSense dropped entirely, top space small enough to crowd or large enough to bring the float back, and centred text inside full-width rows. Drew a control tower icon rather than borrowing one, because neither lucide reference says it alone: lighthouse reads as coast and rescue, radio-tower reads as broadcast with no place anyone sits. The union is a structure with a CABIN and SIGNAL, and the cabin does the identifying: a wide glass box on a narrow stem is the silhouette of an airport tower and of nothing else, and with it present the arcs stop meaning radio and start meaning in contact with the floor. ## Session: 2026-09-14 12:20
- **Branch:** main
- **Files changed:** DEVLOG.md, frontend/src/components/EventCredit.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx
- **New files:** frontend/src/hooks/useDeviceClass.js
- **Notes:** TASK-80. Stan asked how the platform senses the device, and the honest answer was that it did not: "For this device" was a fixed string over a hardcoded Control Tower, so opening Home on a phone told the reader that the desktop workspace was the one for their phone. Now detected. The signal is pointer: coarse, not width, because width answers how much room there is, which is the right question for layout and the wrong one here: a desktop window dragged narrow is still a desktop with a mouse, and a tablet in landscape is still a touch device at 1024px. User-agent sniffing was rejected as a stale list of strings that browsers actively lie in, answering what the software is called rather than how it is being touched. Detection changes the ORDER AND LABELS only, never what is available: both groups always render, so a wrong guess on a touchscreen laptop costs a little scrolling rather than access. On handheld the floor group leads with Goods In as the hero and the second rule reads "In the office". Testing it caught a real subtlety: matchMedia returns a NEW MediaQueryList per call, so dispatching a synthetic change event on a freshly created one never reaches the hook's listener, and the first test looked like a failure of working code; stubbing matchMedia and remounting through the router proved it properly. Also caught a single card stranded at half width in the two-column grid on handheld, so a Group component now uses the grid only when there are two cards to put in it. - **Notes:** TASK-85, part 1 of the inventory history work. New inventory_history table: one row per SKU per month, LONG format, which is the decision that lets it carry any number of months since the header never changes and only the row count grows. No closing_value column, it is closing_qty * unit_cost_sgd derived in SQL, because storing it would be a third place for the same number; unit_cost_sgd IS stored per row since cost at that time is a point-in-time fact. Seed extended from 26 to 105 weeks of sales so every one of the 24 periods has real transactions behind its issues figure, and buildHistory walks BACKWARDS from the known on-hand so the chain ties exactly rather than approximately: closing of the newest period is the actual stock, issues are summed from fulfilled sales, and only receipts are constructed, clamped so opening never goes negative. A verifyHistory() tie-out runs at the end of every seed and fails loudly: every period must balance, every opening must equal the prior closing, and the newest closing must equal inventory_positions.on_hand_qty. Two PRNG bugs found and fixed by measuring rather than reasoning. The shared mulberry32 stream is threaded through every SKU in order, so drawing extra numbers anywhere shifts every later SKU: extending the sales tail moved GMROI 0.68 to 0.67 and DIO 106 to 108, and passing the shared rng into buildHistory moved them again. Both now use per-SKU streams seeded from the SKU id. Verified by diffing the last 95 days of sales against a temp DB seeded from the pre-change generator: byte-for-byte identical, and all nine headline KPIs plus health counts and exception count are unchanged. History total at the newest period is SGD 3,711,450, which equals stats.totalInventoryValue exactly, so the chart cannot disagree with the KPI strip.

- **Notes:** TASK-96, making explanation errors structurally hard and caught when they happen, done on free llama3 and fake-model harnesses only (no paid calls, ledger unchanged at USD 0.0164). Three layers. (1) Each alert type's trigger comparison is written by the system as the opening sentence ("X was flagged because its inventory position of 230 MT ... is at or below the approved reorder point of 250 MT"), and the separate placeholders it is built from are withheld, so a model cannot re-pair them; Sonnet's "inventory position has reached 250 MT" is now unwritable. A first version offered the clause as a required {alert_trigger} placeholder, which failed the format so often on llama3 that 28 of 32 answers fell back to free text at 2.91 calls, so the system writes it instead. Placeholder lists were trimmed per alert type after reading answers: min_order and safety_stock off stockout and reorder ("order at least that amount" against a suggested 597), overstock_amount off everything (IDLE wrote "write down the 18 MT" when 78 MT was idle), supplier and lead_time off IDLE and AGEING. (2) src/llm/semantic.js moves the benchmark's checks into production, folded into runExplanation as dangerous issues (retry, then reject to the rule-based trace): positional "A exceeds B by C" arithmetic, low-stock alerts described as full and ordering advised on too-much-stock alerts (with negation, including "before placing"), a figure attached to a named quantity (inventory position, on-hand, available, maximum, reorder point) matching its real value, a quantity attached to ordering matching the suggested order ("reorder 20 MT" when 597), and any claim that an action was already taken ("the supplier has been informed"), which contradicts the approve-every-action design. Tested against the real failures, correct sentences, all 16 engine-written alert texts (0 flagged) and 128 saved benchmark answers, where every remaining flag was a real error. (3) Two causes of wasted retries found by recording WHY attempts were rejected: every product name contains a digit ("Japonica Short Grain 5KG"), so the no-digits rule rejected answers that merely named the product, which is most of why llama3 needed 2 to 3 calls; product names and KG pack sizes are now exempt while figures are still rejected. And 15 placeholder values ("SGD $26.2K", "1 month and 23 days") held figures absent from the facts list, so the verifier called correct answers invented; it now also knows every value the system inserts (0 of 122 flagged; an invented 999 MT still caught). Also: any brace token that is not an exact placeholder name is rejected ({Product} used to reach the reader), ".:" joins and a doubled "was flagged because" are cleaned, and "Here's another attempt:" lead-ins are stripped. Result on llama3, 8 alerts including the reorder scenario, 4 passes: first-try acceptance 0% to 94%, model calls per explanation 2.59 to 1.06, placeholder path 13 to 32 of 32, free-text fallbacks 19 to 0, contradictions shown 3 to 0, latency 10.6 s to 4.3 s. On the paid tier 1.06 calls is under half the cost per explanation. A fake model deliberately making each known mistake is blocked in every case, a contradiction on every attempt is never shown and its billed calls are still counted, and a good answer for each of the six alert types is accepted on the first call. Ready for the Sonnet check Stan asked to hold until morning.

While preparing the formula review for Stan, one more combination was checked: with the spec's sales and cover rules together, TJ-10KG's cover falls to 43.7 days against a 45 day lead time, but its open PO lands in 20 days, so it stays covered and no alert is added.

- **Notes:** Stan is bringing a teammate onto the repo and asked for a workflow that prevents the usual two-person merge problems: pushing straight to main, and starting work on a stale branch. Two layers, same split as the existing docs-check.sh: enforcement in plain git hooks that fire for anyone on any tool, reminders in Claude Code and Kiro hooks that only fire inside an agent session. The pre-commit secret scan lived only in .git/hooks, never pushed, so the teammate would not have had it either; moved to .githooks/pre-commit (tracked) with a branch guard added ahead of the existing scan, refusing a commit made on main. .githooks/pre-push refuses a push whose remote ref is main, PRs only. .githooks/post-checkout fetches origin/main quietly on branch creation and says if the branch point is stale. All three wired in by `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`, now the last step of `npm run install:all` so a fresh clone gets them the same way; the old local-only .git/hooks/pre-commit was removed since the tracked one supersedes it. Reminder layer: .kiro/hooks/branch-check.sh, one script for both a Claude Code SessionStart hook and a Kiro PostTaskExecution hook, prints if the session is on main or behind origin/main, never blocks. .gitattributes marks DEVLOG.md merge=union, since two people appending a session entry each is not a real conflict. Tested by hand: a commit attempted on main was refused with the branch guard message; a commit with a planted API_KEY=... line on a feature branch was still refused by the secret scan; both a normal commit and a normal push on a feature branch go through. GitHub branch protection on main (require pull requests, block direct pushes) was recommended and Stan agreed, but gh is not installed here, so that step is a one-time click-through in GitHub's web UI rather than a file change, given to Stan directly. rules.md gained a Working rules entry for the branch workflow and a Tooling entry for the new hooks; handoff.md's file tree gained a line for .githooks/. ## Session: 2026-09-17 (MVP2 Day 2 of 7)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-forecast-day1
- **New files:** backend/src/engines/forecast.js, backend/scripts/bench-forecast.js
- **Notes:** The forecast engine itself, per the plan's locked shortlist (naive-seasonal, linear-trend on a deseasonalized series, Holt-Winters), all Node-only and deterministic, no ML dependency. Holt-Winters' alpha/beta/gamma are fit by a coarse grid search minimizing in-sample SSE (never hand-set), and it degrades to naive-seasonal below two full seasonal cycles of history rather than fit unstable parameters on too little data. `backtest()` is the deterministic auto-mode selector: rolling-origin walk-forward, origin starting at month 12 so Holt-Winters sees one full season, scored by WMAPE (not plain MAPE, which blows up on an Idle SKU's near-zero months) — no model call anywhere in it. `bench-forecast.js` calls `backtest()` directly, the same function the recompute API route will call in Day 3, matching `bench-models.js`'s own rule that the score has to describe the real path. Run against the real seed: 9 of 10 SKUs score WMAPE 0.10-0.25 (10-25% error) with `naive_seasonal` winning most and `linear_trend` winning TJ-25KG; JP-5KG (the Idle SKU, one sale in 24 months) correctly comes back low-confidence with 0 scored folds rather than a false-precision score. Verified by hand, not just by reading the code: TJ-25KG's naive-seasonal October forecast is the average of the two Octobers in its 25-month series, (144.5 + 158.2) / 2 = 151.35 which rounds to 151.4 exactly as the script printed (confirmed the apparent 151.3 vs 151.4 mismatch in a first manual check was Python's own float-precision rounding of 151.35, not an engine bug, by recomputing with Decimal); the 6-month `avg_daily_demand_forecast` (146.65 MT mean / 30 = 4.89) also matched by hand. `/30`, not the calendar-exact days per month, to stay comparable with `avg_daily_usage_30d` (`velocity.js`), which uses the same convention. Not yet wired into `engines/index.js` or `safetystock.js` — that's Day 3. check-formulas.js unaffected, as expected; nothing in the existing engines was touched today. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (MVP2 Day 3 of 7 — the centerpiece)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-forecast-day1
- **New files:** backend/src/engines/riskbuffer.js
- **Files changed:** backend/src/engines/index.js, backend/src/engines/forecast.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, backend/scripts/check-formulas.js
- **Notes:** Forecast and risk buffer wired into the real safety-stock path. `engines/index.js`: for a `use_forecast=1` SKU with an active `forecasts` row, `avgDailyDemand`/`demandCv` going into King's formula (safetystock.js, untouched) come from the forecast instead of `v.avg_daily_usage_30d` — but ONLY inside that one call; `avg_daily_usage_30d` itself and its other seven consumers are untouched, preserving the "one demand rate" decision on record in design.md. Risk buffer is a separate additive term after King's formula runs, not folded into the variance math, so the formula stays provably unchanged and the buffer stays explainable on its own ("+12 MT for India export-ban exposure") rather than disappearing into the Z*sigma number. `strategic_adjustment` (the documented Phase 2 placeholder) is the analytics-output field the risk buffer lands in, overriding the raw stored column rather than writing back to it — a computed figure, not stored state. New engine `riskbuffer.js`: deterministic SQL match on `risk_events` by country_of_origin or supplier, capped at 30 days, no LLM involved anywhere. New output fields: `demand_source`, `forecast_avg_daily_demand`, `forecast_demand_cv`, `forecast_low_confidence`, `risk_buffer_mt`, `risk_buffer_days`, `risk_buffer_reason`, `reorder_point_suggested_with_risk`.

  Four new routes: `GET /api/forecast/models` (the 3 models + auto, shaped like `GET /api/llm/mode`), `PUT /api/skus/:id/forecast-config` (a separate action from the general SKU edit on purpose, so forecast_model/use_forecast can never move as a side effect of an unrelated Save), `POST /api/forecast/recompute` (runs for every SKU with `forecast_model` set, not gated on `use_forecast` — picking a model is intent, the switch is separate activation; 'auto' runs the real backtest and writes its winner, a pinned model runs without one), `GET /api/skus/:id/forecast` (the active row, decoded, for the panel and the approval rationale later). This is the lazy-cache write side the "no scheduler" recompute mechanism needs — `engines/index.js` only ever reads the active row, never recomputes inline.

  Verified live, not just read: `PUT .../forecast-config` then `POST /api/forecast/recompute` on TJ-25KG picked `linear_trend` via a real backtest (matching Day 2's CLI benchmark exactly), and `GET /api/skus` showed the safety-stock numbers actually move (58.06 -> 50.77 MT, `demand_source` flipping from `velocity_30d` to the forecast) while `avg_daily_usage_30d` stayed byte-identical before and after, confirming the isolation holds under a real HTTP round-trip, not just a direct engine call. The risk buffer showed up correctly too: TJ-25KG (Thailand) picked up the seeded Thailand logistics event, `reorder_point_suggested_with_risk` (298.1) exceeding `reorder_point_suggested` (252.54) by exactly 10 days x the demand rate in force.

  check-formulas.js extended with 6 new checks, all proven to actually fail before trusting them (this repo's own rule, after a past miss): `forecast_naive_seasonal` and `forecast_linear_trend` are independently re-derived from raw `sales_transactions`, never calling the engine on the spec side. Holt-Winters gets a property check instead of a second implementation (every forecast month within `[0, 3x max historical monthly demand]`) — re-implementing exponential smoothing a second time would mostly test whether the same bug was made twice, not catch a real one. `risk_buffer_mt` and `reorder_point_suggested_with_risk` are simple re-derivations. `backtest_determinism` runs `backtest()` twice on identical data and requires byte-identical WMAPE — the one check guarding the "deterministic, not agentic" property this whole feature's legitimacy rests on. Proved two of the six by hand: temporarily capped the risk buffer at 5 days instead of 30 (caught, 8 of 10 SKUs differ) and injected `Math.random()` into `backtest()`'s scores (caught, 10 of 10 differ) — both reverted after confirming, all 31 checks pass clean on the real seed. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (MVP2 Day 4 of 7 — the other centerpiece's front door)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-forecast-day1
- **Files changed:** backend/src/engines/alerts.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, backend/scripts/check-formulas.js, frontend/src/pages/Alerts.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Activity.jsx
- **Notes:** Step 6 of Stan's Reorder Loop, built exactly as planned: no new "pending" state on `decisions`, `generateAlerts()` gains a `POLICY_CHANGE_SUGGESTED` alert type (severity info, only for a `use_forecast` SKU whose `reorder_point_suggested_with_risk` drifts more than 10% from the approved `reorder_point_policy`), reusing the entire existing alert lifecycle and `ApprovalModal`/`POST /api/decisions` flow. Extracted `applySkuUpdate(db, skuId, fields)` out of `PUT /api/skus/:id` so approving a policy change calls the exact same function, in the same transaction as the `decisions` insert, rather than a second write path — reject applies nothing. `ApprovalModal` gained a rationale branch (forecast model, low-confidence flag, risk buffer reason, the suggested reorder point) sourced from the SKU's own already-computed fields, nothing re-derived in the frontend. Activity's `DECISION_RECORDED` case now names the applied change ("Approved reorder point is now 43 MT") when one happened.

  **A real bug, caught by testing through the actual UI rather than trusting a curl request I built by hand.** My first curl test passed `trigger_type` directly and worked; the backend's `applyPolicy` check also read `b.trigger_type` directly — but `Alerts.jsx`'s `handleDecision` sends the field as `alert_type`, never `trigger_type` (matching the existing DB-insert line just above it, which already handled this with `b.alert_type || b.trigger_type`). So a real approval through the real modal silently recorded the decision and applied nothing: `reorder_point_policy` stayed at 85 when it should have moved to 43, with no error anywhere — the UI said "Approved" and the database just didn't move. Caught by checking the SKU's actual value after clicking through the real modal, not by reading the code or trusting the curl-based check. Fixed by resolving `triggerType` once (`b.alert_type || b.trigger_type`) and using that single value for both the insert and the apply-policy check, rather than two separate reads of the request body that could disagree. Re-verified the full loop through the real UI after the fix: filtered Alerts to "Policy Suggestion," opened Basmati Premium 5KG's card, confirmed the modal showed "Forecast model: naive seasonal," "Risk buffer: +10.9 MT (India non-basmati export restriction (2023-style), illustrative)," "Suggested reorder point: 85 → 43 MT," clicked Approve, and confirmed `reorder_point_policy` actually became 43, the audit log recorded `policy_applied: {reorder_point_policy: 43}`, and Activity's sentence read correctly. Also checked the reject path applies nothing (confirmed: policy unchanged).

  `check-formulas.js`'s existing `alerts` check re-derives all alert rules independently, so it needed the same >10% gap rule added or it would have started failing the moment any SKU had `use_forecast` on — added. All 32 checks pass on a fresh seed; frontend build passes. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (MVP2 Day 7 — demo mode, with Stan present)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-forecast-day1
- **New files:** backend/src/routes/demo.js, frontend/src/components/DemoModeBadge.jsx
- **Files changed:** backend/src/db/init.js, backend/src/index.js, backend/src/engines/financials.js, frontend/src/App.jsx, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx
- **Notes:** The one item deliberately held back from the unattended session — Stan back and present for it, as agreed. Confirmed two things before touching anything: stay on the current branch (not a stacked one — this is still all MVP2, matching how Days 1-7 have lived on one branch so far), and no PIN/login gate on the toggle (Stan is the one demoing this live, and it can never reach the real database regardless of who clicks it).

  Built exactly to the plan's spec. `db/init.js` gets an `AsyncLocalStorage` (`demoContext`) and a single shared `demoDb` reference (or `null` — no demo session has started, or one was exited and dropped). `getDb()` checks `demoContext.getStore()` first and returns that instance if present, falling back to the real singleton otherwise — every existing call site in every engine and route needed zero changes, since none of them thread a request object through to `getDb()` in the first place; that indirection is the entire point of using AsyncLocalStorage here instead of, say, a global flag. `initDb()` gained an optional `targetDb` parameter so the exact same schema-building code runs against either the real file or a fresh `:memory:` instance — `createDemoDb()` is three lines because of it. `enterDemoMode()`/`exitDemoMode()`/`isDemoModeActive()` manage the one shared instance; `runInDemoContext()` is what the middleware calls per request, and self-heals (creates a fresh empty instance) if the shared one was dropped since the visitor's cookie was set, rather than silently falling back to the real database for someone who believes they're in a sandbox — the one failure mode this feature exists to make structurally impossible, not just unlikely.

  Three small routes (`routes/demo.js`): `POST /demo/enter` and `POST /demo/exit` set/clear a plain `stocksense_demo=1` cookie (no signing needed — it carries no sensitive data, just a boolean, and the worst case of forging it is entering an empty sandbox), `GET /demo/status` for the frontend badge to check on load. One middleware line in `index.js`, added after `express.json()` and before every route: reads the cookie, wraps `next()` in `runInDemoContext()` if present. Requires no cookie-parser dependency — Express's `res.cookie()`/`res.clearCookie()` need no middleware to set cookies, only to *parse* `req.cookies` conveniently, and reading one specific cookie by hand from `req.headers.cookie` is three lines.

  **Verified the isolation directly before wiring in any HTTP plumbing** — the highest-risk part of tonight's work, so checked first and in isolation: a Node script entered demo mode, inserted a SKU inside the demo context, and confirmed the real database's count stayed at 10 throughout, both inside and after the demo context closed. Then the same thing again over real HTTP with curl and a cookie jar: `/api/skus` returns 0 with the cookie, 10 without it, at every point in an enter → check → exit sequence, in either order.

  Frontend: `DemoModeBadge.jsx`, a fixed top-right control mounted once at the App root (`App.jsx`) rather than duplicated into Home and Layout — shows "Enter demo mode" or "DEMO MODE — Exit" depending on `GET /demo/status`, and reloads the page (`window.location.href = "/"`) on either action, deliberately: nothing in this app shares a data cache across pages, so a hard reload is the simplest way to guarantee every page re-fetches against whichever database is now active rather than showing stale state from the one just swapped out from under it. Mounted on Home and every Control Tower page, not the handheld — same reasoning Layout.jsx already gives for keeping the warehouse floor chrome-free.

  **Two real, pre-existing bugs found — not by demo mode's own mechanism, which worked correctly the first time, but by finally exercising a genuinely empty portfolio, which nothing in this app had ever done before** (the real seed always has 10 SKUs). `Dashboard.jsx` rendered "nulld of supply" and two "null% of inventory" strings. Root cause, once traced: `financials.js`'s `overstockPct` and `eoPct`, plus `healthByValue`'s per-status `pct`, all divided by `totalInventoryValue` with no guard against zero — `0/0` is `NaN`, and `res.json()`'s `JSON.stringify` silently turns a `NaN` into a bare `null` on the wire (NaN isn't valid JSON), which is why the frontend saw `null` rather than `NaN`. Fixed by guarding all three the same way `turnover`/`gmroi`/`dio` two lines above them already were (`totalInventoryValue > 0 ? ... : 0`) — the inconsistency was that three of six near-identical calculations had the guard and three didn't. `dio` itself was correctly `null` by design already (an empty portfolio genuinely has no turnover figure) — that one just needed the frontend's template string to handle the null case (`"no turnover yet"`) instead of interpolating it blindly, since `${null}d of supply` is exactly the kind of silent string-coercion bug `rules.md`'s "Number(null) is 0" lesson is about, just the string-template cousin of it rather than the arithmetic one.

  Verified for real in the browser, not just the fix in isolation: entered demo mode, confirmed Inventory ("0 of 0 SKUs · all healthy"), Dashboard (every KPI, the health-by-value breakdown, and the ABC×Movement matrix, all rendering cleanly at zero rather than crashing or showing null/NaN), and Alerts ("No active alerts") all handle a genuinely empty portfolio gracefully; exited, confirmed all 10 real SKUs came back exactly as they were. Console clean on every page, both in and out of demo mode. `check-formulas.js` (all 30 checks, unaffected — the three guards are no-ops on the real seed where `totalInventoryValue` is always positive) and `npx vite build` both pass. No paid calls.

  Known limitation, not fixed tonight: entering demo mode lands on an empty Inventory table, not an onboarding flow, since the onboarding work (`Onboarding.jsx`, the empty-state redirect on Home) lives on the separate `feature/mvp2-onboarding` branch, not this one. Worth deciding before a live demo: merge that branch in first, or accept an empty table as the demo-mode landing state for now.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (a fourth forecast model, ported from a teammate's branch)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-forecast-day1
- **Files changed:** backend/src/engines/forecast.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, backend/scripts/check-formulas.js, frontend/src/pages/ForecastDetail.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan asked to actually integrate the teammate's (Tawmo, via Kiro) independent forecast engine flagged earlier tonight — read their real code first (`design.md` and `forecast.js` on `origin/feature/demand-forecast-engine`) rather than promising blind. Their method: a damped Holt level+trend (fixed alpha/beta/phi, not fitted per SKU — ~24 points would overfit three parameters) blended 50/50 with a seasonal-naive term scaled to the current level, backtested with rolling-origin MAPE/MAE. Genuinely different math from the three already here, well-reasoned, properly documented — not a duplicate.

  Ported the algorithm as a fourth entry in `MODELS`, not merged wholesale, per the plan discussed earlier: `MODEL_IDS`, the backtest, and `forecast_model`'s valid values are all derived from that one array, so adding a model is one array entry, not a rewrite. Two adaptations, both to make it a fair fourth option rather than a copy-paste: (1) their original read `inventory_history.issues_qty` directly; this version takes the same `monthlySeries()` series every other model already reads (`sales_transactions`, fulfilled-only), so all four models compare on one definition of demand, not two — the exact bug class `design.md` already documents a real incident from. The underlying numbers barely move (both sources track fulfilled outflow), only the plumbing changed. (2) their daily-rate conversion divided by a calendar-exact 30.44; dropped, since `runForecast()` already divides by a flat 30 for every model to stay comparable with `avg_daily_usage_30d` — needed no rate conversion of its own once plugged into the shared wrapper. `demand_cv_forecast` likewise needed no separate porting: the existing generic calculation in `runForecast()` already runs on whatever model's output arrives, uniformly.

  `check-formulas.js` got a fifth forecast check, `forecast_holt_damped_seasonal_bounds`, a property check (every forecast month within `[0, 3x max historical monthly demand]`) rather than a second implementation — same reasoning already on record for Holt-Winters: re-implementing a smoothing method a second time mostly tests whether the same bug was made twice. `backtest_determinism` needed no change; it already iterates `MODEL_IDS` dynamically rather than a hardcoded list of three, so the fourth model was covered automatically. Frontend: added the model to `MODEL_ORDER`/`MODEL_COLOR` (purple, the one unused categorical color already in the palette) and `MODEL_HINT` in `ForecastDetail.jsx`, plus its label and description in `GET /api/forecast/models`.

  Verified for real, not just that it compiles: ran it directly through `runForecast()` first (real numbers, real backtest score) before touching the UI at all. Then in the browser — the fourth card renders correctly (purple, "—" for WMAPE until backtested, matching the other three's own first-run state), switching to Manual and picking it triggers a real recompute and draws a visibly distinct forecast curve, and switching back to Auto correctly re-ran the backtest across all four and picked the same winner as before (naive_seasonal, WMAPE 0.15, beating the new model's 0.29 on this SKU) — the addition doesn't change which model wins when it isn't actually the best one. Console clean. `check-formulas.js` (31 checks; the same 2 pre-existing date-drift failures noted in the previous entry, unrelated) and `npx vite build` both pass. No paid calls.

  Not yet done: `feature/demand-forecast-engine` itself is untouched — only the forecasting math was ported, not their branch's other changes (they also touched `db/init.js`, `seed.js`, and independently drafted decisions in `docs/(Stan) 2 To review/` about replace-vs-blend and a trust threshold, which are Stan's calls and separate from this).

- **Notes:** Pushed the branch and opened PR #2 (Stan, from his phone — `gh` couldn't authenticate from this sandbox, both attempts hit a consistent network timeout on GitHub's device-login endpoint specifically, everything else reachable, so this wasn't retried a third time; gave Stan the pre-filled GitHub compare link instead, which worked fine from mobile Safari).   Both were real, not noise. `ForecastDetail.jsx:304`: `barPct = Math.max(6, Math.round((bestScore/score)*100))` divides by zero when `score === 0` — a backtest with a perfect fold-by-fold prediction, `bestScore/score` becomes `0/0 = NaN`, which renders as an invalid `"NaN%"` CSS width and breaks that model's score bar entirely. Fixed: since `bestScore` is a `min()`, `score === 0` always implies `bestScore === 0` too, so that case is now a flat 100% bar instead of a division. `design.md:409`: the "Forecast-Driven Demand & Risk Buffer" section's model list was written earlier tonight, before the fourth model (`holt_damped_seasonal`) was added in a later commit, and never went back to include it — added, with the same attribution to Tawmo already in `forecast.js`'s comments.

Those 4 live as inline annotations on the "Files changed" tab, a different view from the review-body screenshots Stan had access to on mobile; asked him to check that tab when convenient rather than guess at what they say. `npx vite build` passes; `check-formulas.js` still shows the same 2 pre-existing date-drift failures from earlier tonight (BR-10KG, unrelated, already investigated). - **Branch:** feature/mvp2-onboarding-day8 (branched from main; this work was accidentally started while

- **Notes:** Stan chose to build Day 8 from the MVP2 plan's stretch list — after a fresh catalog/sales upload, a new onboarding step 3 suggests a target service level (by ABC tier), a target stock (demand over review period + lead time, plus safety stock), and an honest "no receiving history yet" note on lead time, instead of leaving every new SKU at raw schema defaults.

  `engines/onboardingSuggestions.js` reads `getAnalytics()`'s own fields throughout (`avg_daily_usage_30d`, `safety_stock_mt`, `abc_class`, `lead_time_days`) rather than recomputing any of them — same "derived value computed twice" discipline as everywhere else in this codebase. `REVIEW_PERIOD_DAYS` was previously duplicated as a local constant inside `routes/inventory.js`'s forecast-preview route; moved to this new engine file and imported, so there's one definition, not two.

  Two new routes: `GET /api/onboarding/suggested-settings` (never writes) and `POST /api/onboarding/suggested-settings/apply`, which applies only the SKUs a manager kept via `applySkuUpdate` — the same function `PUT /api/skus/:id` uses, so a batch accept and a hand edit share one write path. Frontend: `SuggestedSettingsReview.jsx` (new), a genuinely different shape from `ImportPreview`'s field-diff (these are policy suggestions with a confidence signal, not changes already read from a file), reusing `Panel`/`Stat` where they fit. Wired into `Onboarding.jsx` as step 3; both the sales-history upload path and its "Skip for now" now route into this step instead of straight to `/`.

  **Human-in-the-loop moment, by design**: the ABC-tier service-level defaults (A/B/C) are a product call, not a technical one, so I built the whole pipeline against placeholder values and asked Stan to set the real numbers rather than picking silently. Stan asked for either my recommendation or a researched, cited source for a help tooltip — did both: found the one genuinely relevant peer-reviewed paper (Teunter, Babai & Syntetos, "ABC Classification: Service Levels and Inventory Costs," *Production and Operations Management*, 2010) via web search, and it turned out to be a caveat, not an endorsement: its actual finding is that fixed per-tier service levels (the exact practice being built) leave real savings on the table versus full cost-based optimization. Presented that honestly rather than citing it as support for the numbers. Landed on A: 98%, B: 95%, C: 90% (mid-range of common FMCG practitioner convention, ~97-99%/93-96%/88-92%, sourced from several industry blogs, weaker evidence than the peer-reviewed paper and labeled as such). Built an IEEE-format citation with a working DOI link into a `HoverHint` tooltip (reused the existing component, not a new one) next to the suggestion — had to explicitly style the `<a>` tag, since this app resets all links to `color: inherit; text-decoration: none` globally (`index.css:300`), which would have made the citation link invisible.

  Stan then asked for the tooltip to follow real progressive-disclosure convention rather than dumping the full paragraph and citation on hover at once (the Wikipedia-footnote / Perplexity-citation-chip pattern: a short claim with a small `[1]` marker, full source collapsed until asked for). Rebuilt the tooltip body around a native `<details>/<summary>` toggle ("Show source") instead of a JS-driven accordion, since it needs no extra state and degrades gracefully. Verified expand/collapse live in the browser, not just that it built.

  Found and fixed one real bug via the browser, not caught by the build: `api.getSuggestedSettings()` resolves directly to the array (the shared `request()` helper already unwraps `{success, data}` to just `data`), but the first version of `enterReviewStep` read `res.data`, throwing "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')" the moment step 3 loaded. `npx vite build` passed cleanly both before and after the fix — a bundler can't catch a wrong object shape at a call site, only real interaction can. Fixed, reverified via full click-through (upload skip → step 3 renders 10 real suggestions → toggle a checkbox → Apply → values persist in the actual database → lands on Home), then reseeded.

  `design.md` gained a new "Onboarding Suggested Settings" formulas section (target_service_level_suggested, target_stock_suggested, lead_time_days_suggested), consistent with "every formula lives in design.md." `check-formulas.js` unchanged (no new engine formula needing an independent check — this reads existing checked fields, doesn't derive new ones) and reruns clean. `npx vite build` passes. No paid calls.

- **Notes:** Stan was using demo mode to test things without touching the real database and hit two real gaps.

  1. **"Try with sample data" on the onboarding screen, demo-mode only.** The onboarding comment used to say this shortcut was "deliberately NOT built" against the real catalog, because reseeding wipes decisions and the audit trail — a real, destructive action a stray click shouldn't trigger. That reasoning doesn't apply inside demo mode's already-disposable in-memory sandbox, so it's a different decision there, not a reversal of the old one. New `POST /api/demo/seed-sample` reuses `db/seed.js`'s existing `seed()` unchanged — it already calls `getDb()`, which resolves to whichever database the current request is running against, so running it inside the demo AsyncLocalStorage context seeds the sandbox with zero new plumbing. The one thing genuinely new: `isInDemoContext()` in `db/init.js`, which checks whether *this* request is inside the demo context (not just whether demo mode is active for someone else) — the route refuses with 403 unless that's true, so it can never wipe the real database even called directly with no cookie. Verified with curl and in the browser: real database's SKU count unchanged after seeding the sandbox twice; the button seeds and lands on a fully populated Dashboard still showing "DEMO MODE — Exit".

  2. **Cancelling "Add one product by hand" on an empty catalog stranded the user.** Onboarding hands off to `/inventory?add=1`, which opens the same Add SKU modal a manual click would. Cancelling it just closed the modal, leaving a bare empty Inventory table with no path back into onboarding — Home.jsx already renders Onboarding automatically when the live SKU count is 0, but that check lives only on the Home route, not Inventory. Fixed by having the modal's close/cancel handlers check `skus.length === 0` and `navigate("/")` in that case, reusing Home's existing empty-catalog behavior rather than duplicating an empty-state check in Inventory.jsx. Applies to both real and demo onboarding — the same stranding can happen in the real flow, not just the demo one Stan was testing. Verified both branches live: cancelling on an empty catalog now returns to Onboarding; cancelling on a 10-SKU catalog still just closes the modal and stays on Inventory, unchanged.

  Also fixed along the way: `BigButton` in `Onboarding.jsx` hardcoded "Reading…" as its busy-state label regardless of context, which would have shown the wrong text for "Loading sample data…" — added a separate `disabled` prop (matching the same fix already made in `SuggestedSettingsReview.jsx` this session) so a caller can gate a button without inheriting an unrelated label.

  **A persistent screen-edge glow while in demo mode** — the same idea as a Zoom screen-share border, so a manager navigating anywhere in the app can't lose track of being in the sandbox. Added to `DemoModeBadge.jsx` (the one component that already owns "is demo active" and mounts at the right spots) as a static, non-interactive `inset` box-shadow — deliberately not pulsing, restraint over a distracting animation on something being shown to someone. Verified live on Home and Dashboard: persists across navigation, doesn't intercept clicks.

  **Onboarding minimize/resume**, scoped down from Stan's original ask. He described a full multi-page guided tour with overlays pointing at specific UI elements, pause/resume/rewind — a real "movie theater" experience. Laid out the honest cost (real hours of new engineering, doesn't move any of the four judged deliverables, 11 days out from shortlisting) and asked him to choose a scope; he picked the smaller slice: a minimize button on the wizard plus a reopenable "Continue setup" chip, not cross-page overlays.

  New `lib/onboardingResume.js` holds two localStorage keys (`onboarding:step`, `onboarding:minimized`) behind try/catch, matching `useCollapsed.js`'s existing pattern for private-mode safety, shared between `Onboarding.jsx` and the new `OnboardingResumeChip.jsx` (mounted at the same App-root spots as `DemoModeBadge`, bottom-left so it doesn't compete with the badge or the new glow for a corner). `Onboarding.jsx` reads the saved step on mount so resuming doesn't restart the wizard, and clears the saved state at every existing "done" action (sales-history upload success, both "Skip for now" buttons) so the chip never lingers claiming there's something to continue.

  Hit and fixed one real conflict: minimizing from step 1 (an empty catalog) writes the flag then needs to land on Home — but Home already auto-shows Onboarding whenever the catalog is empty, which would immediately loop right back and defeat the whole point. Fixed by having Home's empty-catalog check also read `isMinimized()` and skip forcing Onboarding when true — the flag exists specifically to say "I know it's empty, let me look around anyway." Then hit a second, subtler bug from the same root cause differently: `minimize()` originally called `navigate("/")`, but when the wizard is already showing (via Home's own inline render at `/`), that's a no-op to React Router since the path doesn't change, so Home never re-renders to notice the new flag. Fixed by using a full `window.location.href = "/"` reload instead, the same pattern `trySampleData` above it in the same file already uses for the identical "state changed underneath us" reason. Both bugs only showed up through actual browser testing — screenshots that looked like nothing happened, not a build or console error.

  Verified live end-to-end: minimize at step 1 (empty catalog) → lands on Home's real workspace picker with the chip showing → chip resumes at step 1. Minimize at step 2 (simulated via localStorage) → resumes at step 2 correctly. "Skip for now" at step 2 clears both keys and correctly falls through to Onboarding again, since the catalog is still genuinely empty at that point — not a bug, the honest outcome when there's truly nothing to explore yet.

  `check-formulas.js` and `npx vite build` both pass. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (a design tuner for the demo-mode glow)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux (same branch, pushed after the previous entry)
- **Files changed:** frontend/tuners/demo-glow.html (new), frontend/tuners/README.md
- **Notes:** Stan wanted the demo-mode glow (added earlier this session) more diffused and breathing rather than a static blue strip, and asked for a tuner in the same style as `type-scale.html`/`glass-tooltip.html` rather than describing the look in words, plus a straight answer on whether animating it costs anything on the hosting side.

  Built `demo-glow.html` following the existing tuners' exact conventions (no `<!doctype>`/`<html>`/`<body>`, Google Fonts preconnect, light/dark token blocks, versioned localStorage key, a real mock Control Tower behind the glow rather than an empty box, Copy CSS button). Controls: ring (the thin solid edge), diffusion (blur), thickness (spread), opacity min/max and cycle length for the breathe, plus four presets from "As shipped (static)" to "Unmissable."

  Answered the cost question directly rather than just building the tool: zero hosting cost either way, since this is pure client-side CSS that never touches the API, database, or the Lightsail container. The real (client-side) cost is kept near-zero by animating opacity only, one of the few CSS properties a browser animates on the compositor thread with no repaint or layout, rather than animating the shadow's blur/spread values directly, which would force a full-viewport repaint every frame. Built the tuner's own apply() function to actually work this way (fixed box-shadow, only opacity/animation change), not just claim it in the note text, so the generated CSS block is honest about what it hands back.

  Caught two writing-rule violations before shipping, not after: several literal em dashes in the file's own prose and JS comments (this repo's rules.md bans em/en dashes everywhere, including code comments) rendered as mojibake once served without a charset meta, exactly the failure mode the tuners' own README already warns about for non-ASCII characters. Replaced all seven with commas, colons or parentheses per house style, then re-verified rendering clean in a real browser (served locally via python3 -m http.server, since the browser tool can't navigate file:// URLs directly) rather than trusting the fix by inspection.

  Not yet applied to DemoModeBadge.jsx itself: Stan is tuning it first and will send back the generated CSS block. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (Apple Intelligence-style rainbow glow, replacing the flat one)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux (same branch, pushed after the previous entry)
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/components/DemoModeBadge.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan said the flat breathing glow wasn't landing and asked for something like Apple Intelligence's edge glow on iOS: a rainbow gradient, specifically rainbow in dark mode. This is a different technique from anything tuned so far, not another slider nudge on the same box-shadow, so built it directly rather than routing back through demo-glow.html.

  The standard "gradient border" CSS trick: a fixed, full-viewport div with `padding` defining the ring thickness, masked with two gradient layers XOR'd together (`mask-composite: exclude`) so only the padding ring stays visible, with a rotating `conic-gradient` painted underneath via a child element sized to `inset: -50%` so full rotation never reveals a gap at the corners. Colour stops are iOS's own system colour sets (blue, purple, pink, orange, yellow, green, teal) for light and dark, not an invented rainbow, so it reads as genuinely "Apple's" rather than approximate. Only `transform` (the rotation) and `opacity` (a gentle breathing pulse layered on top) animate, both compositor-only properties, so the cost per frame stays the same as the flat version despite looking far more elaborate - the mask, the gradient stops and the blur are all static once drawn.

  Hit a real bug on the first attempt, not a build error: `content-box` is only a valid token inside the `mask` SHORTHAND property, never inside `mask-image` alone. Writing it into `WebkitMaskImage`/`maskImage` silently dropped the whole malformed declaration rather than erroring, so nothing was masked at all and the rainbow gradient painted across the entire viewport, badly hurting readability. `npx vite build` passed cleanly through this the whole time, since it's syntactically valid JS/CSS, just semantically wrong CSS the browser silently ignores - only opening it in a real browser showed the actual problem. Fixed by moving the declaration into the `mask`/`WebkitMask` shorthand instead.

  Verified live in both themes: light gets the softer iOS-light stop set, dark gets the fully saturated iOS-dark set; two screenshots three seconds apart in light mode showed the colour positions had visibly rotated around the ring, real evidence the animation is cycling; confirmed on Dashboard too (persists across navigation, page content and sidebar fully readable, nothing intercepts clicks). `check-formulas.js` and `npx vite build` both pass. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (a real layout bug in the new banner, and a tuner for what to do about it)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux (same branch, pushed after the previous entry)
- **Files changed:** frontend/tuners/demo-frame.html (new), frontend/tuners/README.md
- **Notes:** Stan sent a screenshot of the just-shipped banner and found two real problems: the sidebar's bottom items (workspace switcher, "Running on StockSense" footer) were clipped by the browser window, and he asked for an interactive mockup to choose the layout rather than another round of chat description.

  Diagnosed the clip: `Layout.jsx`'s sidebar and main both size themselves to `100vh` independently, and the banner's `padding-top` on `<body>` adds height on top of that without either child knowing, so the total content is taller than the actual visible window and the bottom of the sidebar runs off-screen. A real bug, not a one-off - any layout claiming a full viewport height stacks badly under a banner that reserves its own space at the top.

  Stan proposed the actual fix himself: instead of overlaying a strip and accounting for it everywhere, shrink the app's own content area so the indicator is structural (a frame around the window) rather than an overlay competing for space. Built `demo-frame.html` to compare that against the current banner and a third option (a minimized strip that expands on hover) side by side, using a fixed-height mock browser window so the clipping bug reproduces in the tool exactly like it did in the real screenshot, rather than an infinitely-tall page that would hide it. Controls: which approach, its thickness, label alignment, and which side the Exit button sits on.

  All three modes confirmed what the diagnosis predicted: full banner clips whenever content is tall enough to already fill the window; the minimized strip only reserves a negligible 10px so it barely clips but leaves Exit unreachable without hovering first; the frame clips nothing by construction, since the app's content area is inset by the frame's thickness on every side rather than sized independently.

  `emit()` here hands back a plain text description (mode, thickness, alignment, exit side) rather than paste-in CSS, since the three approaches need different code shapes - the frame's fix specifically touches `Layout.jsx`'s sizing, not just `DemoModeBadge.jsx`, so building it needs to happen in code, not by pasting a style block.

  Caught the exact charset bug the tuners README already documents before it shipped: the mock sidebar's Chinese brand text rendered as mojibake once served without a charset meta, the same failure `brand.html`'s own build script exists to prevent. Fixed by escaping it as numeric HTML entities, matching that established convention, and reverified rendering clean in a real browser.

  Also hit a tooling artifact worth recording so it is not mistaken for a real bug later: the browser automation tool's hover coordinates twice landed on the wrong element (a scroll-position mismatch between the screenshot and the page's actual layout), making the minimized strip look unresponsive to hover. Verified the underlying interaction logic directly instead (calling the element's own `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` handlers), confirmed it expands and collapses correctly, and did not chase a bug that was not there.

  No code shipped yet for the actual fix - waiting on Stan to pick a mode from the tuner. `check-formulas.js` unaffected (docs-only change). No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (demo banner rebuilt as a structural top frame, fixing the real clip)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux (same branch, pushed after the previous entry)
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/index.css, frontend/src/components/DemoModeBadge.jsx, frontend/src/components/Layout.jsx, frontend/src/components/Sidebar.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx, frontend/tuners/README.md
- **Notes:** Stan picked the frame from `demo-frame.html`, with one clarification the tuner's three-way comparison hadn't offered: top edge only, not all four sides, since the point was never a picture-frame look, it was making the app's real content area structurally smaller so the label can never overlap it.

  Grepped every `100vh` in the frontend rather than guessing which ones mattered: five real spots (`Layout.jsx` x2, `Sidebar.jsx`, `Onboarding.jsx`, `Home.jsx`) assume the full viewport height, plus one in `index.css`'s `.hh-screen` that's correctly left alone, since `DemoModeBadge` never mounts on the handheld warehouse routes. Added `--demo-banner-height` as a plain layout token in `index.css` (`:root { --demo-banner-height: 0px; }`, deliberately outside the light/dark theme blocks since it isn't a colour), and `body { padding-top: var(--demo-banner-height); }` so every page shifts below the fixed banner without needing to know it exists. Then changed all five `100vh` spots to `calc(100vh - var(--demo-banner-height))`, so the same one variable both pushes content down (via body's padding) and shrinks it to fit (via every layout's own height calc) at once. `DemoModeBadge.jsx` now sets that one CSS variable via `document.documentElement.style.setProperty` (and removes it on exit) instead of reaching into `body.style.paddingTop` directly, which was the shape of the original bug: padding shifted content down without any of the height assumptions underneath it shrinking to match, so total content ran taller than the visible window and the sidebar's own bottom items ran off-screen.

  Verified the fix is sound by construction, not just at one window size: banner height plus `calc(100vh - banner height)` always equals exactly `100vh` regardless of the actual viewport, so it isn't a magic number tuned to one screen. Confirmed live anyway: read `--demo-banner-height` and `body`'s computed `padding-top` directly (both `40px` while active, both `0px` after Exit, no leftover state), and confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the sidebar's real last line of text sits comfortably inside the window rather than past its edge. Noted one separate, pre-existing cosmetic overlap while testing: the onboarding "Continue setup" resume chip (bottom-left, unrelated fixed element from an earlier session) visually sits on top of the sidebar's own footer text at a small window height - flagged to Stan, not fixed here, since it's a different bug from the one asked about.

  `check-formulas.js` and `npx vite build` both pass. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (onboarding preview reachable from Home; catalog + sales combinable in one upload)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js
- **Notes:** Two separate points Stan raised after using the rebuilt onboarding. First: the only way to preview it once the catalog isn't empty was Home → Control Tower → Settings → "Preview the onboarding journey" - three screens deep, and only reachable from inside the Control Tower at all. Added the same link to Home itself, demo-mode only (`api.getDemoStatus()`, same gate `DemoModeBadge` already uses), right under the footer credit - one click now, and it stays off the real database exactly as before.

  Second, and more involved: "Try with sample data" jumps straight past step 2, which read as broken. It isn't - `POST /demo/seed-sample` runs the exact same `seed()` the real database uses, which already includes ~180 days of sales history and 24 months of inventory history alongside the 10 SKUs, so there is no separate "step 2 data" left once it's run. Stan's first idea was flipping the order - ask for sales data, derive the catalog from it - which turned out to have a real hole: a sales row (`sku_id, quantity_mt, sale_date, customer, channel, status`) carries none of the catalog's own fields, not even `product_name`, which `POST /skus/import-new` requires to create anything. Auto-creating a SKU from a sale would mean a reorder policy, safety stock % and min/max stock that all default to 0 - the exact "confident zero" bug class `rules.md` already documents, since a SKU with a policy of 0 reads as healthy everywhere in the app, not as unconfigured.

  Recommended instead: keep the catalog-first order the schema already assumes, but let someone with both files ready finish in one step instead of two. Built that into the catalog upload's own review modal (`Onboarding.jsx`): after picking the catalog CSV, "Also add sales history now (optional)" opens a second file input, previewed via the SAME `POST /skus/history/import-sales` endpoint BulkEdit and step 2 already use, unchanged - not a new combined CSV format, since there is genuinely nothing to combine the two INTO.

  The one real wrinkle: that preview endpoint checks `sku_id` against the live `skus` table, and the catalog upload hasn't been applied yet at preview time, so every SKU in it would preview as "not a known SKU". Added an optional `pendingSkus` array (`[{sku_id, product_name}]`) to the route, honoured ONLY when `apply` is false - verified this by hand with `curl`, not just by reading the code: the same request with `apply:true` for a SKU that was never actually created still correctly rejects it as unknown, so a request-supplied ID can loosen what a dry-run preview SHOWS but never what a real insert accepts. `Onboarding.jsx` applies the two as two sequential real requests (catalog, then sales), not one transaction; if sales fails after the catalog succeeds, the products stay (they're already real) and the flow drops into the ordinary step 2 instead of losing that progress.

  Verified the full path live: uploaded a one-row catalog CSV and a two-row sales CSV for the same new SKU through the actual file inputs, confirmed the modal previews both together with the real pending product name (not a placeholder) in the sales breakdown, clicked "Add 1 product and 2 sales", and confirmed via the API that both landed - the SKU in `/api/skus`, both sale rows in the sales export - and that the flow went straight to Home, skipping step 2, all inside the demo sandbox.

  `npx vite build` and a `node -c` syntax check on the backend route both pass. `check-formulas.js` unaffected (no engine changes). No paid calls.
