## Session: 2026-09-17 (Forecast Detail's plain-English "Data Story"; closed the lead-time-variability edit gap; found and fixed a stale-preview bug)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/pages/ForecastDetail.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Inventory.jsx, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, .kiro/specs/mvp1-inventory-visibility/design.md
- **Notes:** Stan asked exactly how lead time, its variability, target service level and target stock are derived, and wanted tooltips plus a plain-English "here's what we found, here's what we suggest" summary up top, with a way to jump into the model picker / simulation from it. Answered the factual question precisely before building anything, since the honest answer had a correction in it worth surfacing: lead time and its variability are NOT computed from sales data anywhere in this app - they're supplier characteristics, either saved on the SKU or defaulted, and the sandbox already says so ("this app has no closed purchase-order data to measure a real average from"). Target stock is the one field with a genuine live formula behind it. Confirmed two scope calls with Stan before touching code: close the real gap that lead time variability had NO save path anywhere (yes), and where the new narrative should live (a new panel above the existing sections, not a rewrite of the reasoning chain).

  Added `DataStory` to `ForecastDetail.jsx`: reads the exact same `preview` object `ReasoningChain` and `ReorderSimulation` already consume (never recomputes anything, so it can't disagree with the numbers below it), states the forecast, the saved lead time and its variability labelled as inputs rather than findings, the suggested reorder point and target stock, the gap against what's currently approved, and an explicit "don't just take these numbers" line naming exactly which inputs are supplied rather than derived. Three buttons scroll to the model picker, the sandbox, and the simulation (same `scrollIntoView` + `prefers-reduced-motion` pattern as Dashboard's Needs Attention jump). Added a `ColHint` to each of the sandbox's four sliders (Lead time, Lead time variability, Target service level, Target stock), explaining what each feeds into King's formula and whether it's an input or a suggestion - the page already had this ⓘ pattern on the chart and the simulation, just not on the sandbox itself.

  Closed the lead-time-variability gap: `lead_time_std_days` was a real DB column (default 0) with genuinely no way for a user to ever set it - not in the CSV import, not in the SKU edit form, only ever touched by the sandbox's own non-saving preview. Added it to `Inventory.jsx`'s `SkuEditForm` (`EDIT_GROUPS` + `SLIDER_SPECS`, both fully generic so this was two array entries, not new logic) and to the backend's `SKU_TABLE_FIELDS` / `NONNEGATIVE_NUMERIC_FIELDS` whitelists (`PUT /api/skus/:id`). Deliberately left `AddSkuForm` and the CSV import untouched - Stan's ask was specifically the edit form, and `AddSkuForm` already omits `target_service_level` too, an existing asymmetry not introduced here.

  Found a real bug live, not by inspection: clicking the page's own Recompute button left the new Data Story panel (and, it turns out, the pre-existing reasoning chain and sandbox) stuck showing "Computing…" until a full page reload. Cause: the debounced preview effect only depended on `[inputs, skuId]`, and Recompute changes the active forecast without touching `inputs` at all, so the effect never re-ran to fetch a fresh preview against the forecast that just became available - it kept whatever `preview` (often a "no forecast yet" error) was set before Recompute was clicked. Added `forecastData?.forecast?.generated_at` to the dependency array. This is a real UX fix that predates this session's Data Story work but was much more visible with a big highlighted panel sitting on it, which is exactly how it was found.

  Verified live end to end: recomputed a fresh SKU's forecast and confirmed Data Story populated immediately, no reload needed; hovered a sandbox tooltip and confirmed the What is this?/How to read it panel renders; clicked all three Data Story buttons and confirmed each scrolled to the right section; opened the SKU edit form's Policy tab, confirmed the new Lead time variability slider appears beside Lead time, changed it, saved, and confirmed via the browser's own `fetch` (not a separate curl session, which turned out to hold a stale demo cookie) that the new value persisted.

  `npx vite build` passes. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js` passes (28/28) - no formula changed, only what fields can be set and how a page explains them. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (Forecast Detail: "not AI, statistical" made explicit; a real rules.md violation caught and fixed)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/pages/ForecastDetail.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Home.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx, frontend/src/components/Sidebar.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx, frontend/src/lib/forecastNudge.js, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, .kiro/specs/mvp1-inventory-visibility/design.md, docs/(Stan) 1 Reference/(Stan) SUBMISSION TRACKER.md
- **Notes:** Stan asked to label Forecast Detail's suggestions as "AI generated" so a reader knows the reorder point isn't a plain calculation. Pushed back rather than agreeing, since he asked explicitly not to just agree: nothing on that page is AI-generated. The demand forecast comes from a backtested statistical model (Naive seasonal, Linear trend, Holt-Winters, Holt damped + seasonal, engines/forecast.js), and everything after it is fixed arithmetic (King's formula). This app's own stated architecture is "deterministic engines compute every figure, a language model only explains, never computes," and the ONE place a real LLM touches anything is the Why? button on Alerts, bounded to narration. Found direct precedent for the correction in this exact codebase: Alerts.jsx's own AI modal disclaimer used to say "AI-generated analysis" and was already corrected once, with a comment explaining it "overstated what this actually is." Labelling Forecast Detail as AI would reintroduce that exact mistake somewhere new, and undermines the "Architecture & Reasoning Loop" / "Autonomy & Human-in-the-Loop" story the whole submission is built around.

  Proposed the honest version of what Stan actually wanted (visibility into what's input vs. computed vs. model output) and he agreed to it: split the reasoning chain's single "derived" tag into three - YOUR INPUT (blue, unchanged), STATISTICAL FORECAST (new, purple, the demand number specifically), and no tag at all for plain formula output (safety stock, risk buffer), so the two things worth flagging keep their colour and fixed arithmetic doesn't compete for attention. Added a ColHint on the reasoning chain's own header stating plainly "None of this is generative AI" and naming where the real one lives.

  Caught a real mistake of my own while reviewing this: several new sentences I'd written this session (the Data Story panel's own prose, sandbox tooltips, a few code comments, design.md's Forecast Detail section) used em dashes, which `rules.md` bans outright in new writing - Stan flagged this once already as an "AI vibes" tell and I should have been checking against it as I went, not after four separate features. Audited every file touched this session against that rule, distinguishing my own new prose from pre-existing text (which the rule doesn't require rewriting) by cross-checking against what each file looked like before my edits, and rewrote every sentence that was actually mine into hyphens, commas, colons or two sentences instead. Left every pre-existing em dash in place, including several in this exact session's own earlier commits, since those predate the writing this fix was about.

  Verified live: recomputed a fresh forecast and confirmed the reasoning chain shows STATISTICAL FORECAST / YOUR INPUT / FORMULA correctly on the right steps, and confirmed the new header tooltip renders the "none of this is generative AI" text. Re-ran `npx vite build` and a backend syntax check after the dash cleanup to confirm the rewrites didn't break anything structurally.

  `npx vite build` passes. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js` passes (28/28). No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (blank catalog template ships a filled-in example row, with a safety net against it being imported for real)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/components/ImportPreview.jsx
- **Notes:** Working through the onboarding flow, Stan downloaded the blank catalog template and found 21 bare column headers with no sense of what belonged in most of them, then asked why onboarding demands so much beyond simple sales data. Traced it in the code first rather than guessing: the DB schema (`db/init.js`) only makes `sku_id` and `product_name` NOT NULL, and the import routes (`/skus/import`, `/skus/import-new`) only require those same two - every other column (lead time, safety stock %, reorder policy, unit cost, and so on) already has a sensible default and is genuinely optional. The gap was that the template didn't show that distinction; it handed over every editable column undifferentiated.

  Discussed two ways to close that gap: an in-app reference page explaining each column, or a filled-in example row shipped inside the template itself. Recommended the example row as the more durable of the two, since a separate reference page can drift out of sync with the columns as fields are added or renamed, while an example row generated from the same code that defines the columns cannot. Stan agreed to build that first and revisit a reference page afterward.

  `/api/skus/export` now ships one realistic example row (Thai Jasmine 25KG, sku_id `EXAMPLE-DELETE-ME`) instead of bare headers when the catalog is empty. Because a first-time uploader can plausibly forget to delete it before importing, `/api/skus/import-new` now recognises that exact sku_id and skips it rather than creating a real SKU, surfaced as a non-blocking warning ("the example row was skipped, not added") rather than a hard error - real rows in the same file still import normally. `/api/skus/import` (the update path, for existing SKUs) needed no change: it already rejects any sku_id it doesn't recognise, so a stray example row there was already safe. Along the way, generalised `ImportPreview.jsx`'s warning panel, whose body text was hardcoded to the monthly-history import's specific wording (the only existing caller) - it now reads `preview.warningBody` per import type, defaulting to that same text so the history import's behaviour is unchanged.

  Verified against a throwaway scratch backend instance (separate port, separate `DATA_DIR`, never touching the real dev DB or its 10-SKU demo data): confirmed the export endpoint returns the example row only when the catalog is empty; confirmed posting that row unmodified to `/skus/import-new` with `apply: true` creates nothing and returns the warning; confirmed a real new SKU uploaded in the same file alongside the leftover example row still gets created while the example row is dropped.

  No formula or engine change, so `check-formulas.js` doesn't apply here. No paid calls. Still open, per Stan: whether to also build the in-app reference page with per-column explanations and examples.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (the catalog fields reference page, and a real zero-default bug it would otherwise have documented as correct)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/App.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx, frontend/src/pages/CatalogFields.jsx (new)
- **Notes:** Follow-up to the previous entry: Stan asked to go ahead with the reference page after all, wanting column-by-column elaborations with examples, reachable from the onboarding screen.

  Building it required knowing the actual default for every column, and re-checking those against `POST /skus` (the single "Add SKU" form) surfaced a real bug rather than just a documentation gap: `/skus/import-new`'s per-cell loop defaulted EVERY blank numeric cell to 0, including `safety_stock_pct` and `lead_time_days`, while `POST /skus` correctly defaults those two to 20% and 45 days (matching `db/init.js`'s own column defaults). Verified live on a throwaway scratch backend: a CSV-imported SKU with those two cells left blank came back as 0% safety stock, 0-day lead time, and GREEN (healthy) regardless - the exact "`Number(null)` is `0`, and 0 is finite" pattern `rules.md` already names three prior bugs from, a fourth instance here. Writing "leave this blank for a sensible default" on a reference page while that was true would have been actively wrong, so fixed it first: added a small `CREATE_NUMERIC_DEFAULTS` map (`{ safety_stock_pct: 20, lead_time_days: 45 }`) next to `CREATE_NUMERIC`, consulted with `?? 0` in the blank-cell branch. Re-verified on the same scratch instance: blank now yields 20 / 45, while a genuinely zero-appropriate field like `min_order_qty` still defaults to 0. Confirmed against a stash of the change that the two `check-formulas.js` failures seen afterward (`days_of_cover`, `target_days_of_cover` on BR-10KG) are pre-existing date drift, not caused by this fix - both formulas depend on `today()` against seed data's fixed `received_days_ago`, and the same two failures appear with the fix fully reverted.

  New page `CatalogFields.jsx` at `/onboarding/catalog-fields`, linked from Onboarding step 1 next to the template download button ("What does each column mean?"). States for each of the 21 template columns: required or not, the actual default if left blank, a plain-English meaning, and a realistic example - grouped under Identity / Inventory Policy / Costs, reusing `Inventory.jsx`'s own `EDIT_GROUPS` section titles rather than inventing new vocabulary for the same fields. A separate closing section names the five columns (`on_hand_qty`, `reserved_qty`, `quality_hold_qty`, `lead_time_std_days`, `target_service_level`) that appear in the downloaded template - because it doubles as the existing-catalog export - but are silently ignored by `/skus/import-new` when creating a brand-new product, since a new SKU always starts at zero stock; explains where to set them instead (Inventory, or Goods In). Also corrected Onboarding step 1's own footer copy, which overstated the requirement the same way the blank template did (it listed `rice_variety`, `country_of_origin`, `packaging_size` and `supplier` as needed, none of which actually are) - now states plainly that only `sku_id` and `product_name` are required.

  Deliberately not auto-generated from the backend's column lists: those are the contract (what's accepted), this page is the explanation (what a sane value looks like and why), and keeping them as two things to update by hand mirrors how `requirements.md` and `design.md` already stay separate rather than one generated from the other.

  Verified live in the browser (light theme, demo-mode sandbox, real dev server on 4000 untouched throughout): the reference page renders all four sections correctly, "Back to setup" returns to `/onboarding`, and the new link from step 1 navigates to the reference page and back. `npx vite build` passes. No paid calls.

  Same-session follow-up: Stan noticed several fields carry a unit (MT, %, days, SGD) that was only mentioned in the "Example" line, easy to skim past. Considered renaming the actual CSV columns to embed the unit (`min_stock_mt`) but ruled it out - the import parser matches column names by exact string (`SKU_TABLE_FIELDS`, `IMPORT_COLUMNS`), so that would break every already-downloaded template and touch a dozen-plus files for a cosmetic gain. Instead added a small unit badge directly beside each field's code name in `CatalogFields.jsx` (`min_stock` `MT`), reusing the `unit` value each field entry already carried - no backend or data-model change, no import contract touched. Verified live: badges render correctly (MT, %, days, SGD / MT) on every field that has one, blank on the identity/text fields that don't.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (what sales-only data can and can't compute; then an "Assumed" badge in the upload review so the gap is visible, not silent)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/components/ImportPreview.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan asked, out of curiosity, whether a customer's sales history alone could let StockSense infer their inventory policy, hypothetically making the whole Inventory Policy section optional. Rather than answer from first principles, ran the actual experiment on a throwaway scratch backend (separate port, separate `DATA_DIR`, real dev DB on 4000 confirmed untouched before and after both times this session): created a SKU with only `sku_id` and `product_name`, fed it 120 days of synthetic sales (50 transactions), and read back what the engines actually produced.

  Findings, grounded in the live output rather than the formulas alone: demand-side figures (`avg_daily_usage_30d`, `demand_cv`, `movement_class`, `abc_class`/`xyz_class`, `velocity_trend`) come entirely from sales, no catalog input needed. But `reorder_point_suggested` and `safety_stock_mt`, though they look like grounded recommendations, are quietly built on `lead_time_days: 45` and `target_service_level: 0.95` - the silent defaults, not anything sales data could supply, since a sales row records what was sold to a customer, not how long a supplier takes to deliver. Also found that `design.md`'s `target_stock_suggested` formula (line 485) is only implemented inside the Forecast Detail sandbox preview (`inventory.js:1230`) - it never appears on the main SKU record at all, sales history or not. And `reorder_point_policy`/`target_stock` never auto-fill from the suggested figures regardless of how much sales history exists, by design - the health status that actually alerts a manager came back RED on this test SKU, but for an unrelated reason (0 on-hand stock, since a brand-new SKU always starts there).

  Stan's read on this, unprompted: he'd been assuming lead time and service level got the same treatment as everything else, and was surprised the defaults were invisible in the upload flow - proposed showing what got assumed right in the preview table, same session, before asking whether it was a good idea. Recommended building it into the existing `ImportPreview` review step rather than a new screen, and flagging only `safety_stock_pct`/`lead_time_days` rather than every defaulted field, since a 0 for `min_stock` isn't an assumption worth a human's attention the way a fabricated lead time is - Stan agreed and asked to go ahead, "whatever's industry standard."

  Landing this is the same "one source of truth" pattern as the reference page: `ASSUMPTION_LABELS` sits beside `CREATE_NUMERIC_DEFAULTS` in `inventory.js` so the badge's text can never drift from the number actually written. `/skus/import-new`'s per-row loop now collects which of the two flagged fields were blank into `assumed`, returned per row in the preview's `changes` array. `ImportPreview.jsx` renders each as a small yellow pill ("Assumed 45-day lead time") beneath the row - reused the app's own `--yellow`/`--yellow-light` tokens (checked `index.css` first; there is no `--amber`) rather than inventing a new color for "this is a standing assumption, not your data," which is exactly what YELLOW already means elsewhere (health status, ageing).

  Verified live in the browser (demo-mode sandbox, real dev server confirmed untouched again afterward): uploaded a bare-minimum spreadsheet (`sku_id`, `product_name`, `unit_cost_sgd`, `unit_price_sgd` only) through Onboarding step 1 and confirmed both "Assumed 20% safety stock" and "Assumed 45-day lead time" badges render on the review row before Apply. `npx vite build` passes, backend module loads cleanly. No paid calls.

  Same-session follow-up: to let Stan compare the bare-minimum and fully-detailed cases himself, first tried handing him two scratch CSV files to upload through his own file picker - which doesn't work, since the scratchpad directory is this session's machine, not his, and his browser's file picker can't reach it. Drove the upload through the browser extension directly instead (works regardless of that boundary, already proven earlier this session), which answered the immediate question but isn't something Stan can repeat himself later. So added it to the app for real: two built-in examples (`EXAMPLE_CSVS` in `Onboarding.jsx`, one bare `sku_id`/`product_name` pair, one with all 17 editable columns filled) reachable as "Or try an example: bare minimum · everything filled in", demo-mode-gated the same way "Try with sample data" already is. Both go through the exact same `previewText` call a real file pick uses (factored `onFile` down to a shared helper first, so a built-in example and an uploaded file can never diverge in how they're handled) and land in the same review modal - no new UI pattern, no shortcut around the real import path.

  Verified live: clicked "bare minimum" and confirmed the two Assumed badges appear per row exactly as the manual test showed; clicked "everything filled in" and confirmed no badges appear since nothing was left blank; cancelled both without applying. Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. `npx vite build` passes. No paid calls.

  Same-session, one more gap Stan spotted immediately after: step 2 (sales history) had none of what step 1 just got - no example, no quick add. Couldn't reuse the same fixed-CSV approach, though: a sales row's `sku_id` has to match a product that actually exists (`/skus/history/import-sales` rejects anything it doesn't recognise), and which products exist at step 2 depends on whichever path was used in step 1 - a real upload, either example, "Try with sample data", or one added by hand. A hardcoded sales CSV would only work by coincidence.

  So `trySalesExample` fetches the live catalog (`api.getSkus()`) and `buildExampleSalesCsv` generates 90 days of sales for up to the first 3 SKUs found, seeded off each `sku_id` (a small linear congruential generator, not `Math.random`) so re-clicking against the same catalog produces the same numbers rather than a new random set each time - a demo that changes every click reads as broken, not helpful. Surfaced as "Or generate example sales for my products" beneath the Upload button, demo-mode-gated like everything else in this family of shortcuts. - **Notes:** Stan, clicking through the demo catalog he'd just built (EXAMPLE-BARE-01), asked why the "What your data tells us" panel said 201 MT while the page header said SUGGESTED 189 MT - he couldn't find 189 anywhere in the text. Both SKUs default to `use_forecast: 0`. So for any SKU that hasn't been switched over, the header and the Data Story panel will structurally disagree - correct behaviour, wrong presentation: the panel states its number in the same flat declarative voice as the header, with nothing telling a reader these are two different bases.

  Gave Stan three options (explain the gap, show both demand rates side by side, or something else); he picked the explanation. Added a conditional paragraph to `DataStory`, `!sku.use_forecast` gated (the exact condition under which the two figures CAN diverge - when `use_forecast` is on they already agree), naming both demand rates by number and pointing at "See the model behind this" below, which already scrolls to the existing `use_forecast` checkbox in `ReasoningChain` (`ForecastDetail.jsx:651`, "Use this forecast for safety stock & reorder point") - reused that control rather than adding a second toggle. ## Session: 2026-09-18 (a real crash in `linearTrendForecast`, found while Stan was just clicking Recompute)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/engines/forecast.js
- **Notes:** Stan hit "Failed to recompute forecasts. Retry." on the demo sandbox and asked why. Reproduced rather than guessed: called `POST /api/forecast/recompute` directly from the browser console (`javascript_tool`, so it ran with the real demo cookie against the actual in-memory sandbox) and got a consistent 500, not a one-off - three attempts in a row, same SKU. Isolated it to the SKU's `forecast_model`: pinning `EXAMPLE-BARE-01` (which recomputes fine on `auto`) to `linear_trend` reproduced the exact same failure, so this wasn't specific to one demo SKU's data - `linear_trend` itself was broken for short sales histories.

  Root cause in `linearTrendForecast` (`engines/forecast.js:97`): `seasonalBaseline(m)` is called both for historical months (building `points`, always safe - every historical month is by definition a key in `byMonth`) and for TARGET months (the 6-month forecast horizon), which `byMonth` was never built from. With under ~12 months of history, every target month is one never seen in the data, so `byMonth.get(m)` returns `undefined`, and `undefined.reduce(...)` throws. `naiveSeasonalForecast` a few lines up already had the identical case (a target month absent from history) and already handles it (`values?.length ? avg : overallAvg`, `forecast.js:91`) - `linearTrendForecast`'s own comment even describes wanting exactly this fallback ("a month with a zero seasonal baseline... floor it at 1"), but the code only guarded against a baseline of 0, never against the month being absent entirely. Any SKU with a sales history shorter than a full calendar year forecasting a 6-month horizon hits this - not an edge case, the default case for a new product, which is exactly why both fresh demo SKUs (bare-minimum and full-detail alike, both only ~4 months of history) hit it immediately once `linear_trend` was in play, whether picked manually or (rarer, since `auto`'s backtest usually needs more history to even consider it a contender) picked by auto mode.

  Fix: added the same `overallAvg` fallback `naiveSeasonalForecast` already uses, so a target month with no direct history borrows the series' overall average instead of crashing, one line and a comment explaining why it's needed at both call sites now.

  Verified against the freshly nodemon-restarted backend (in-memory demo DB resets on restart, so this was tested against a clean rebuild, not leftover state): recreated the exact failure shape (one new SKU, ~30 sales rows over 90 days, pinned to `linear_trend`) and got a clean 200 with `avg_daily_demand_forecast: 1.92`. Then ran all four models plus `auto` against the same sparse SKU - all five now succeed. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: `forecast_linear_trend` PASSes explicitly; the only two failures are the same pre-existing `days_of_cover`/`target_days_of_cover` date-drift on BR-10KG already confirmed unrelated earlier this session (same two failures reproduce with this fix fully reverted). Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (a real navigation bug: finishing sales in onboarding from Home's inline render silently went nowhere)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/pages/Onboarding.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan reported that clicking "generate example sales for my products," then "Add 69 sales," left him stuck on "Give it something to learn from" - it looked like nothing had happened, and asked why, twice, clearly frustrated. First reproduction attempt (via the standalone `/onboarding` route) worked fine, which was the wrong signal to trust: Stan was reaching onboarding through Home's own inline render (catalog empty, landed at `/` with `<Onboarding/>` mounted inline), not the separate route, and that distinction turned out to be exactly the bug.

  Confirmed via the API directly (not by staring at the screen) that the apply was actually succeeding both times Stan hit it - `sales_90d` for both demo SKUs had roughly doubled, meaning the import genuinely went through underneath a screen that looked frozen. That ruled out a failed request and pointed at the navigation after success instead.

  Root cause, found by grepping this exact file for every place onboarding tries to leave itself: `applyImport`'s two sales-branches (`Onboarding.jsx:249` and `:264`) called plain `navigate("/")`, but `exitOnboarding` and `trySampleData` a few lines above both use `window.location.href = "/"` instead, with a comment already explaining exactly why - "reachable from Home's OWN inline render... so we're already at '/' and a client-side navigate to the same path never re-renders Home to notice the new [state]." The two sales-completion branches were written without applying that same lesson to themselves: when reached via Home's inline render, `navigate("/")` to a URL you're already ON does nothing, so the SPA route never changes, Onboarding never unmounts, and the exact same step-2 screen with its old local state just sits there - a real, pre-existing bug (not introduced this session), just never triggered before because every prior test of this button went through the standalone `/onboarding` route instead of Home's inline path.

  Fix: both branches now use `window.location.href = "/"`, matching `exitOnboarding`/`trySampleData`, with a comment pointing back at the original explanation instead of repeating it.

  Verified by deliberately reproducing the SPECIFIC entry path that exposes the bug, not just any success: reset the demo sandbox to genuinely empty (`POST /demo/exit` then `/demo/enter`, confirmed 0 SKUs via API), navigated to `/` fresh so Home's inline render (not the `/onboarding` route) was in play, added the bare-minimum catalog example for real, generated and applied the example sales - and this time it correctly landed on Home's workspace picker instead of staying stuck. Confirmed both applies actually persisted (`sales_90d` present on both SKUs). Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. `npx vite build` passes. No paid calls.

- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux

Answering the second question honestly surfaced a real bug in the first: `channel` (a category - direct/wholesale/food-service) and `movement_no` (a genuine identifier - GRN-0009) were both sitting under one shared "Reference" label from the previous session's column-alignment pass, which was itself the mistake - aligning position and label without checking whether the underlying data meant the same kind of thing.

  Checked `warehouse.js` directly for when a PO number is genuinely load-bearing rather than guessing: `POST /warehouse/inbound/receive` (line 100-108) hard-refuses to post a receipt without a matching row in `purchase_orders` where `status = 'open'` - a receipt with no real backing PO is not something the real Goods In flow can ever produce. That made the previous session's `PO-2025-####` values a genuine bug, not just a display nit: they were fabricated strings with no matching `purchase_orders` row at all, something the real app could never have created. Stan caught it by asking directly whether the PO number was real, after separately stating his own (correct) understanding that Goods In requires an existing PO to begin with.

  Fixed at the source, not the display: `seed()`'s RECEIPT-generation block now inserts a matching `purchase_orders` row (`status: 'received'`, real `ordered_qty`/`order_date`/`eta`/`actual_arrival`) for every synthetic receipt, using a genuine sequential `PO-2025-####` counter (`histPoSeq`) instead of the earlier `1000 + random*9000` string, which also fixes a real latent bug found while doing this - that random scheme had no uniqueness guarantee at all against `po_number`'s `UNIQUE` constraint, a small but real risk of `seed()` throwing on a collision that just hadn't been hit yet in testing. Kept this counter separate from the existing `poSeq` (which numbers the genuinely still-open, future-incoming POs as `PO-2026-####`) specifically so the year prefix stays meaningful - `PO-2026` still-open, `PO-2025` already-received, not one shared sequence where the number alone can't tell you which.

  Also reversed the previous session's column-sharing decision, per Stan's implicit correction: Date/SKU/Qty stayed aligned (genuinely the same concept both places), but the 4th column now shows what's honestly there instead of forcing one label over two different kinds of data - `Channel` for sales (a category), `PO number` for receipts (now a real identifier, sourced from `reference` instead of `movement_no`).

  Verified in two stages. First, ran `seed()` against a throwaway scratch database and queried the join directly: `SELECT ... FROM goods_movements m JOIN purchase_orders po ON po.po_number = m.reference` returned all 26 receipts matched, `status: 'received'` on every one, 0 unmatched, 0 duplicate `po_number`s - confirming the fix actually closes the gap, not just relabels it. History-balance verification still passed unchanged. Then live in the browser: reset the demo sandbox, clicked "Try with sample data," and confirmed the Goods receipts table now shows real `PO-2025-####` values under "PO NUMBER," matching what the join had already confirmed server-side. Added one new backend endpoint for this rather than deriving it in the frontend from the on-screen top-3 preview rows (which aren't the full data): `GET /skus/opening-balance-suggestions`, one SQL sum per SKU (RECEIPT total minus fulfilled sales total, all time, clamped at 0), registered ahead of the existing `/skus/:id` route - the first version 404'd as "SKU not found" because Express matched the literal path as a `:id` param first, caught by actually curling it rather than trusting the route table by eye. Rendered as a small "Use ~X MT" chip next to each input, clicking it fills the box; the box itself still starts blank.

  After the balance step, a second modal ("Your first quick read") reads straight off `GET /skus`'s already-computed fields - `days_of_cover`, `health_status`, `suggested_order_qty` - nothing recomputed. Left `pickNeedsAttention(skus)` (which SKUs earn a callout on this first screen) as a `TODO(human)` for Stan: a real product decision between several defensible rules (RED health only, days-of-cover-under-lead-time, suggested-order-qty > 0, some combination), not implemented yet.

  Stan then asked for ELI18 tooltips explaining how everything shown is derived. Reused the existing `ColHint`/`HoverHint` pattern (already used for jargon on other pages) rather than inventing a new tooltip component: one on "opening balance" itself in the balance-step intro, a native `title` on each "Use ~X MT" chip explaining it's inferred not counted, and three on the analysis screen's column headers (days of cover, health, suggested order) explaining the formula in plain language.

  Verified: `node -e "require('./backend/src/routes/inventory.js')"` loads cleanly, `npx vite build` passes. New endpoint tested three ways - against the real dev server (correctly all-zero, since that persistent DB predates this session's receipt-seeding work and has no `goods_movements` rows yet, confirmed directly via `better-sqlite3` query, not assumed), against a throwaway scratch DB after running `seed()` fresh (JP-5KG correctly nets to 18.2 MT, the rest correctly clamp to 0 since 24 months of sales volume dwarfs the handful of seeded receipts - expected, not a bug), and via the route-ordering fix itself (curled before and after moving the route above `/skus/:id`). Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-18 (a bank-account bug: sample receipts totaled less than sample sales)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/db/seed.js, backend/src/routes/demo.js
- **Notes:** Stan noticed only 1 of 10 SKUs got a nonzero "Use ~X MT" suggestion in the opening-balance step from the previous change, and reasoned it through correctly rather than accepting it: treat a SKU like a bank account starting at a balance of 0 - you can never spend (sell) more than you've ever deposited (received). If `SUM(receipts) - SUM(sales)` comes out negative for 9 of 10 SKUs, the SUGGESTION formula wasn't the problem, the underlying sample DATA was physically impossible.

  Confirmed the root cause in `seed.js`: the RECEIPT-generation block added earlier this session sized each receipt as "a few MOQs at a time," 1-3 receipts per SKU, completely independent of how much that SKU's 24 months of sales history actually sold - which for a busy SKU is 500-900+ MT, far more than a couple of small receipts could ever cover. Sales and receipts were two uncoupled random processes describing a warehouse that sold rice it never received.

  Fixed by coupling them: captured each SKU's actual total sold (`totalSoldBySku`, summed from the same `sales` array already generated per SKU, no new computation) and sized that SKU's total receipts against it - `targetTotal = totalSold * (1.1 to 1.25)`, so every SKU ends up with a believable positive buffer rather than an exact break-even or a shortfall. Receipt count scales with that target (2-8 receipts) instead of a fixed 1-3, with the last receipt in each SKU's set taking whatever remains so the sum hits the target exactly rather than drifting from independent per-receipt jitter. Receipt dates now bias roughly chronological (earliest receipt furthest back) rather than fully random, so they read as an ongoing replenishment pattern rather than scattered points. Explicitly scoped as an aggregate fix, not a day-by-day simulation: total received now exceeds total sold for every SKU, but a narrow slice mid-window could still theoretically dip low since receipt and sale dates aren't checked against each other point-by-point - flagged in the code comment as a real limit, not hidden.

  Separately, Stan asked to keep the onboarding sample-data walkthrough itself simpler - 5 SKUs instead of 10 - explicitly scoped to this path only after checking: the master `SKUS` array in `seed.js` (used everywhere else, including `check-formulas.js`'s known BR-10KG date-drift exception) stays at 10; only `/demo/seed-sample` now calls a new `trimSampleCatalog(db)` right after `seed()`, deleting every sku_id-bearing table's rows (`inventory_positions`, `sales_transactions`, `purchase_orders`, `alerts_log`, `decisions`, `forecasts`, `sales_orders`, `goods_movements`, `inventory_history`) for everything past the first 5 SKUs by insertion order, then the 5 `skus` rows themselves - deleted outright rather than marked inactive, since sales/receipts/POs have no `active` flag of their own and would otherwise still sum into totals for products the catalog no longer shows. `audit_log` deliberately left untouched (its `sku_id` is nullable; trimming a demo sandbox's audit trail isn't what this was for).

  Verified in three stages. First, reran `seed()` against a fresh scratch database and recomputed the same receipts-minus-sales sum by hand: all 10 SKUs now net positive (71.1 to 679.4 MT), where 9 of 10 were 0 (clamped from negative) before this fix. Second, exercised the real route end to end against the actual demo sandbox mechanism (entered demo mode via `POST /demo/enter`, called `/demo/seed-sample`, checked `GET /skus` and the new opening-balance-suggestions endpoint, then `POST /demo/exit`) rather than a scratch DB alone, since the trim logic lives in the route, not in `seed()` itself: catalog came back at exactly 5 SKUs, all 5 opening-balance suggestions positive, and the real dev server confirmed still at 10 SKUs immediately after exiting the sandbox - the in-memory demo swap never touched it. Third, `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: same 2 pre-existing failures (BR-10KG's `days_of_cover`/`target_days_of_cover` date drift, already diagnosed as unrelated in earlier sessions), nothing new. `npx vite build` passes, both changed backend modules load cleanly. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (Action Items: a new, additive tab - nearest stockout and blind spots)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/App.jsx, frontend/src/components/Sidebar.jsx
- **New files:** frontend/src/pages/ActionItems.jsx
- **Notes:** Built from a short discovery exercise this session: Stan asked Claude to role-play an SME inventory manager being interviewed about what they'd want to see right after uploading data. The answer, in order: what's urgent today, why in one sentence, what to do about it, then the working-capital side (slow movers), then "can I even trust this number yet." That mapped to a proposed new tab, "Action Items" - explicitly additive, not a replacement: Alerts keeps the approve/modify/reject workflow, Activity keeps the audit record, this page only adds a short, sorted "what needs a decision" view on top of both.

  Sketched as ASCII first (near-zero cost compared to a coded page) before writing any component, per Stan's ask to separate "does the shape make sense" from "is the UI polished." Landed on exactly two groups, not the four-bucket version first proposed: **Nearest stockout** (real fulfilment risk, sorted by soonest projected stockout/safety-breach date) and **Blind spots**, named that way over a few candidates ("Not enough data yet" was the runner-up) - SKUs where a figure elsewhere in the app can't be trusted yet, either no sales history (resolves with time) or an onboarded field left at its schema default (a real setup gap). Overstock, slow-moving, idle, and the policy-change-suggested alert deliberately stay off this page - they're a real third kind of thing (working-capital risk, not time-boxed), and forcing them into either group would misrepresent what each group means; they're still fully visible on Alerts.

  Stan asked two follow-up questions before building: how "natural" the Blind Spots copy could be without being AI-generated (answered: a small decision tree of template sentences with real numbers dropped in - the exact same technique already in `alerts.js`'s messages and the Table page's `classify()` notes, not a new pattern), and whether an LLM belongs anywhere in this feature (answered: not for the numbers, sort order, or bucket - that stays 100% rule-based, matching the standing "model never computes a figure" principle; the one legitimate future opening would be an optional, on-demand cross-item summary narrating already-computed facts, same shape as Alerts' Why? button - not built this session, flagged as a real v2 idea, not forced in). Also did a short industry scan before building (Netstock's "manage by exception," Acumatica's named exception types, Oracle's planning-exception tolerance windows) to ground the shape in what real inventory-planning tools already do, rather than inventing a layout from scratch.

  Built `ActionItems.jsx` at `/action-items`, added to Sidebar's permanent nav as "Action Items" (right after Dashboard - a first guess at placement, easy to reorder). No new backend endpoint: `GET /skus` decides Blind Spots entirely (checks `avg_daily_usage_30d`, `target_stock`, `reorder_point_policy` for the schema-default/zero case), and `GET /skus/:id/projection` is called only for SKUs already at RED/ORANGE health (a small N, not all SKUs) to get `first_stockout_date`/`first_safety_breach_date` for the Nearest Stockout sort - reusing the exact same projection engine Step 12 of the domain spec describes and ForecastDetail already calls, not a new calculation.

  Verified live in the browser against the real running sandbox: navigated to `/action-items`, confirmed both groups render with real data (Thai Jasmine 25KG, RED, 28 days to stockout, "Order 591.1 MT" action; Japonica Short Grain 5KG correctly filed under Blind Spots for having no sales history), and confirmed the "Order" action link correctly routes to `/alerts` rather than trying to approve anything itself, preserving the existing Forecast-vs-Alerts separation of duties. Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched. `npx vite build` passes. No paid calls.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (Action Items gets a "Why?" - researching what "AI-readable database" actually means, then building it)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, frontend/src/pages/ActionItems.jsx
- **New files:** backend/src/llm/fieldGlossary.js, backend/src/llm/explainActionItem.js
- **Notes:** Stan wanted Action Items' rows to be explainable on demand ("why will it stock out in 28 days") and asked three real architecture questions first: how would the AI read the database and get context, what tweaks make a database "AI-readable," and should the response format be tightened (ELI18, point form, short sentences).

  Answered from the actual code, not in the abstract: Alerts already has exactly this pipeline (`backend/src/llm/explain.js` + `slots.js`), and it never lets a model touch the database - `buildFacts()`/`buildSlots()` hand it a labelled text block of numbers the engines already computed, and a placeholder-substitution scheme (`{days_of_cover}`, never a literal digit) makes stating an invented figure structurally impossible, not just discouraged. The single most load-bearing move in that file: the SYSTEM writes the sentence that compares two figures (why 28 days, not just that it's 28 days) - a model given both numbers separately kept pairing the wrong ones.

  Stan then asked directly whether mislabeled/unlabeled data was the real problem and to research what makes a database AI-readable. Ran an actual web search rather than answering from priors - confirmed the field's real practice (descriptive naming, attached metadata/comments, a semantic layer the model reads instead of the schema itself) is exactly what `buildFacts()` already does, and that "the LLM has zero knowledge of your schema by default" is the standard framing for why raw DB access was never the design here. The real, concrete finding: column naming is already fine (`on_hand_qty`, not `physical_stock`, per terminology-map.md), but what a figure MEANS was written out by hand in three separate places with no single source - `db/init.js`'s inline SQL comments, the domain doc's Appendix A data dictionary, and `explain.js`'s own hardcoded labels. A "one source of truth" gap (rules.md), just in prose instead of code. Fixed narrowly: `fieldGlossary.js`, a small labelled map the NEW Action Items code reads from - deliberately not used to rewrite explain.js's existing, already-benchmarked labels, since that function's exact wording was tuned against specific observed model failures and a generic rewrite risks re-breaking what's already fixed.

  Built `explainActionItem.js` as a sibling to explain.js, reusing its generic pieces outright (`validateSlotted`/`renderSlots`/`describeSlots` from `slots.js`, `chat`/`resolveTier`/`providerInfo`/`LlmUnavailable` from `provider.js`, `calmTone` from `tone.js`, `EVENTS.LLM_CALL` from `audit.js`) rather than writing a parallel pipeline. Deliberately did NOT port `explain.js`'s `verifyExplanation`/`semanticIssues` (alert-specific contradiction checks tuned against six known failure modes) - pretending to that rigour on day one for two new item kinds would be dishonest; the digit-ban placeholder guarantee alone is still real and load-bearing. New, separate system prompt (`ACTION_ITEM_SYSTEM`) rather than editing explain.js's SYSTEM/SLOT_SYSTEM: those are hard-tuned to "plain prose, no bullets," and Stan's ask here was the opposite (ELI18, point form, short sentences) - a different voice earns its own prompt rather than a flag on the old one. `calmTone`'s existing `URGENT_OK` set is reused unmodified: passing `alert_type: "STOCKOUT_RISK"` for the stockout kind keeps real urgency language (a real stockout IS urgent), while the blind-spot kind gets urgency stripped (a data gap should never sound urgent) - no new logic needed, the gate already existed for exactly this distinction.

  New route `POST /api/action-items/explain` mirrors `/alerts/explain`'s own shape and re-derives everything live from `getAnalytics()` rather than trusting the client about why a row is on the page, same reasoning as that route's own comment (a stale tab must never hand the model outdated evidence). One small, named duplication: `blindSpotReasonServer()` repeats `ActionItems.jsx`'s three-branch `blindSpotReason()` rather than sharing a module across the Node backend and the Vite frontend bundle - flagged in a comment as a real but small risk (three `if` statements, not a formula), not hidden.

  Verified live against real llama3, not assumed: clicked "Why?" on the Nearest Stockout row and got a correct, fully-substituted answer (5.58 MT/day, 160 MT, 45 days, 28 days, all real, zero invented figures) in ELI18 bullets. That same first live run caught a real small-model artifact - a trailing "Note: I've used only placeholders..." self-commentary line - fixed by a stripping rule (`stripSelfCommentary`), same "rule after the fact, not more prompt text" philosophy already established for `tone.js`. Two more artifacts surfaced across two further live re-tests after that fix (a "Here's my attempt..." preamble that slipped past `slots.js`'s existing 50-character preamble-length cap, and a chatty "Let me know if that's okay!" sign-off) - both fixed the same way, confirmed clean on a third live run against the Blind Spots row. Each fix required reseeding the demo sandbox (nodemon restarting on file save wipes the in-memory sandbox mid-test, a known behaviour from earlier sessions, re-confirmed rather than assumed when the first re-test came back "SKU not found").

  Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: same 2 pre-existing failures, nothing new. `npx vite build` passes, all backend modules load cleanly. Every model call this session ran on the local (free) llama3 tier - the server default - never the paid Bedrock tier, and no paid call was made or approved.

## Session: 2026-09-19 ("Ask about your data" - the tool-calling tier Step 14 describes, and five real bugs it surfaced)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/llm/tone.js, backend/src/llm/explainActionItem.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, frontend/src/pages/ActionItems.jsx
- **New files:** backend/src/llm/tools.js, backend/src/llm/askDatabase.js
- **Notes:** Stan asked the natural next question after Action Items' fixed "Why?" button: what if a user wants to ask something the button never anticipated? Answered honestly that the existing design would NOT extend to that - its entire safety guarantee (no digit outside a named placeholder) depends on the question being known in advance so every fact can be pre-loaded. A free-form question needs the model to ask FOR facts mid-conversation, which the domain spec already names: Step 14, "Agent Explanation and Investigation" - read-only tools, never raw stock-table access, never a write. Stan said to build it.

  Built two new files. `tools.js`: three read-only functions matching Step 14's own examples (`sku_facts`, `compare_skus`, `recent_history`), each returning a small labelled fact block from live `getAnalytics()`/the sales table, never a raw row. `askDatabase.js`: the orchestration - a plain-text "CALL: tool_name(args)" protocol rather than a native function-calling API, since the local tier is llama3 through Ollama and every other model-facing feature here already uses a text protocol instead (the placeholder scheme itself is one). The digit-ban guarantee from `explain.js`/`explainActionItem.js` still holds, it just grows during the conversation: each tool call adds freshly-named placeholders instead of the whole set being fixed up front, capped at `MAX_TOOL_CALLS = 2` to bound both cost and how long a question can run. New route `POST /api/ask-database`, new `AskDatabase` API client method, a small "Ask about your data" box added to `ActionItems.jsx` (separate from the row-level "Why?" modal, since this one starts from a blank question, not a known row). `stripSelfCommentary` (added to `explainActionItem.js` earlier this session) moved into `tone.js` and shared, once this file needed the same three fixes rather than a fourth copy.

  Five real bugs found by testing against actual llama3, not four - each fixed the same way this codebase always fixes model-behaviour problems: a deterministic rule after the fact, never a firmer prompt.
  1. **Anchored CALL regex.** The model wrote "To find out why... I'll use the compare_skus tool...\n\nCALL: compare_skus(A, B) Waiting for the results.." - real intent, wrapped in chatter. An anchored `^CALL:...$` match missed it entirely and the whole garbled thing fell through as a "final answer." Fixed by searching for the CALL line anywhere in the text instead of requiring the whole response to be it.
  2. **Digits inside placeholder names.** The first slot-naming scheme prefixed facts "t1_", "t2b_" etc. slots.js's own PLACEHOLDER regex only recognises `[a-z_]+` inside braces, and its digit-ban scan has no way to know a brace-wrapped token is a placeholder name rather than a stated figure - so a placeholder whose OWN name contains a digit fails the exact check meant to wave real placeholders through. The model correctly wrote "{t1_product}" exactly as instructed and got flagged for "writing a figure directly." Fixed by renaming the scheme to ordinal words (one/two/three), which was always an implicit constraint of the existing system, just never written down because no earlier slot name had ever needed a number in it.
  3. **Answering without ever calling a tool.** With zero tool calls made, the model answered "TJ-25KG doesn't exist. JP-5KG doesn't exist." - both false, and nothing had checked either claim. The digit-ban guarantee doesn't catch this: no digit was written, so there was nothing to reject. This is a different failure class from a wrong number - explain.js has a semantic verifier for exactly this kind of thing, deliberately not ported here (see the file's own comment on why), so a cheaper, mechanical rule was added instead: a final answer is refused, with a correction prompt, until at least one real tool call has happened.
  4. **Quoted arguments.** Even after fix 3, the model wrote `CALL: compare_skus("TJ-25KG", "JP-5KG")` with literal quote marks, which survived into the sku_id passed to `tools.js`'s exact-match lookup - so it failed against the real, unquoted "TJ-25KG", the tool correctly reported "not found," and the model repeated that a real SKU didn't exist. Not a hallucination this time, a parsing bug feeding the tool a string that could never have matched anything. Fixed by stripping surrounding quote characters, not just whitespace, from each parsed argument.
  5. (Noted, not yet fixed) The final answer occasionally runs out of room mid-sentence on a two-SKU comparison - likely an output-token limit on the local model call worth raising, flagged as a follow-up rather than chased in this pass.

  Verified live end to end, reseeding the demo sandbox after every backend edit (nodemon restarts wipe the in-memory sandbox mid-test, expected and already documented behaviour from earlier sessions). Final confirmed run: asked "Why is TJ-25KG different from JP-5KG?", the model correctly called `compare_skus`, and the answer cited real, verified figures for both SKUs (240 MT vs 78 MT on hand, 160 MT vs 78 MT available, 5.58 MT/day demand rate vs correctly "unknown" for the SKU with no sales history at all - accurate, since that fact genuinely doesn't exist, not invented). Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: same 2 pre-existing failures, nothing new. `npx vite build` passes, all backend modules load cleanly. Every model call across this entire investigation ran on the local (free) llama3 tier; no paid call was made or approved.

## Session: 2026-09-19 ("doesn't work" on other questions - three more real bugs in Ask about your data)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/llm/tone.js, backend/src/llm/tools.js, backend/src/llm/askDatabase.js
- **Notes:** Stan tried questions of his own against the new tool-calling feature and reported it "doesn't work." Reproduced directly against the backend (curl, bypassing the browser sandbox reset issue entirely) rather than guessing, and found the actual mechanism was worse than the earlier session's testing had caught: "How much stock do we have of BM-5KG?" - a real, existing SKU - came back "Unfortunately, we don't have any information about BM-5KG. It seems that this SKU doesn't exist in our system." Confidently wrong, not a crash.

  Root cause, traced through the raw tool-call log the endpoint already returns: the model's FIRST attempt called `sku_facts("SKU_ID")` - literally copying the placeholder word from the tool's own description (`sku_facts(SKU_ID) - key figures for...`) instead of substituting the real id the owner had typed. That call failed correctly. But the previous session's "you must call a tool before answering" guard (added to stop a different false-claim bug) only checked whether a tool had been ATTEMPTED, not whether one had ever SUCCEEDED - so after the budget of attempts ran out with nothing real found, the model was told "answer now" and guessed "doesn't exist," and the guard let it through because, technically, tools had been called.

  Three fixes, verified by repeatedly re-running the exact question directly against the backend, not just reasoning about the fix:
  1. Every tool description in `tools.js` now ends with a worked example ("Example: sku_facts(TJ-25KG)") - telling the model SKU_ID is a stand-in, not a literal, cut the placeholder-echo failures substantially.
  2. `askDatabase.js` now tracks `successfulToolCalls` separately from `toolCalls` (every attempt, success or failure). The "must look something up first" gate checks the successful count, not the attempt count; if every attempt in the budget fails, it now fails honestly ("Every lookup failed... so there's nothing real to answer from") instead of asking the model to guess.
  3. Added a deterministic pre-scan: before the model runs at all, the owner's own question is checked against the real catalog's SKU ids (a plain substring match, not a guess - the ids are already known), and any match found is handed to the model directly ("SKU ids already found in that question, spelled exactly right: BR-10KG. Use these exact strings..."). This removed the single biggest source of failure - the model no longer has to correctly extract/retype an id it was already given in plain text.

  Two more artifacts caught in the same live re-testing pass and fixed in the shared `stripSelfCommentary` (`tone.js`): a leading "Based on the facts available, here is the final answer:" preamble (a wording variant the existing "Here's...:" rule didn't cover, since it didn't start the sentence), and a trailing "Note that I did not include any dates or numbers in the answer, as per the rules..." meta-commentary (the existing "Note:" rule only matched narrower phrasing). Also raised `MAX_ANSWER_ATTEMPTS` from 2 to 3: the dominant remaining failure (llama3 writing "{days_of_cover} days" when the value already reads "12 days," correctly rejected by validateSlotted every time, never shown to the owner) often just needed one more free, local-tier try to self-correct.

  Net result, measured by repeating the same real question against the live backend before and after: 0 of roughly 6 attempts succeeded before this pass (mix of false claims and outright failures), 3 of 4 succeeded cleanly afterward, and the failing 4th failed HONESTLY with a clear reason rather than a wrong answer - the doubled-unit habit is a real, disclosed residual limitation of the free local model, not silently hidden. Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout (every test hit it directly with read-only GETs/POSTs to `/ask-database`, which never writes). `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: same 2 pre-existing failures, nothing new. `npx vite build` passes, all backend modules load cleanly. Every model call ran on the local (free) llama3 tier; no paid call was made or approved.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (a better free local model for Ask about your data - scoped, not swapped globally)
- **Branch:** feature/onboarding-demo-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/llm/provider.js, backend/src/llm/askDatabase.js
- **Notes:** Stan asked whether a different free local model would do better than llama3 at the tool-substitution task. Checked what was actually available first rather than suggesting a download: `ollama list` showed `llama3.1:8b` already pulled and unused. Tested it directly against raw Ollama (bypassing the app) with the exact substitution prompt before recommending anything: 3 of 3 runs correctly identified the real SKU id, against llama3's frequent literal `"SKU_ID"` echo - a real, measured improvement, not a guess.

  Before switching anything, re-read `backend/.env`'s own comment above `OLLAMA_MODEL=llama3` and found a directly relevant prior decision: `bench-models.js` had already scored llama3 as MORE accurate and faster than llama3.1:8b specifically for Alerts' explanations (82% clean vs 71%, llama3.1 "invented a figure once"). Flipping the global `OLLAMA_MODEL` default would have fixed Ask-Database's problem while silently regressing that already-benchmarked, different feature - flagged this to Stan rather than applying the obvious-looking fix.

  Scoped fix instead: `provider.js`'s `chat()` now accepts an optional `model` override, used only when the local tier is selected, forwarded straight into `callOllama()` (which already spread its args last over its own defaults, so this cost one line). `askDatabase.js` sets its own `ASK_DATABASE_MODEL` (default `llama3.1:8b`, overridable via its own env var) and passes it explicitly on every call. `explain.js`/`explainActionItem.js` pass nothing, so they're untouched and still get llama3, exactly as `bench-models.js` recommended for them. One feature's fix, not a global one.

  Also fixed the model.1:8b-specific quirk found in that same direct test: named-argument syntax, `sku_facts(SKU_ID = 'BR-10KG')` or `SKU_ID => 'BR-10KG'` - correctly identifying the real value, wrapped in syntax the parser didn't expect. Argument parsing in `askDatabase.js` now strips a leading `NAME =`/`NAME =>` prefix before the existing quote-stripping runs.

  Re-testing surfaced one more thing worth fixing properly rather than retrying past: across several live runs, "{one_days_of_cover} days" - doubling a unit the placeholder's value already carries - was by far the dominant failure, sometimes on every retry within a single question, burning the whole attempt budget on one repeated habit. `validateSlotted` was correctly rejecting it every time (nothing wrong ever reached Stan), but that's a lot of wasted local-tier calls for a fixable pattern. Added `stripDoubledUnits()`: before validation, if a `{placeholder}` is immediately followed by a unit word AND that placeholder's own value already ends in that exact unit, the trailing word is silently dropped - the same "collapse a known artifact" move `renderSlots` already makes for doubled full stops and a repeated product name, not a new pattern for this codebase, just applied to a new artifact.

  Verified against the live backend, same question repeated multiple times before and after each change (not a single lucky run): immediately before this session's fixes, "How much stock do we have of BR-10KG?" failed 3 of 3 times (all on the doubled-unit habit). Immediately after, 3 of 4 succeeded cleanly, with real grounded figures across two different tools (`sku_facts` AND `recent_history`, the latter barely exercised successfully before), and the model field in each successful response confirmed `llama3.1:8b` was the one actually answering. Real dev server rechecked at 10 SKUs, untouched throughout - every test hit `/ask-database` directly, which never writes. `node backend/scripts/check-formulas.js`: same 2 pre-existing failures, nothing new. `npx vite build` passes, all backend modules load cleanly. `backend/.env` was NOT touched - the global `OLLAMA_MODEL=llama3` default, and the benchmark that chose it for Alerts, stand exactly as they were. Every model call this session ran on the local (free) tier; no paid call was made or approved.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (Control Tower auto-refresh, so handheld movements show on the desktop)
- **Branch:** feature/goods-out
- **Files changed:** frontend/src/hooks/useLiveRefresh.js (new), frontend/src/pages/Dashboard.jsx, Inventory.jsx, Alerts.jsx, Activity.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan asked whether a scan on the handheld reaches the Control Tower. Checked through the API first: goods in and goods out both change on-hand, reserved, expected incoming, dashboard value and DIO, health status and the audit log, and add a GRN/DN to the movements book. But every page fetched only on mount, so an open desktop showed nothing until a reload. Added `useLiveRefresh(refresh, 15000)`: a timer plus a refresh when the tab becomes visible, paused while the tab is hidden. Each page passes a SILENT refresh (replace data in place, swallow errors, keep filters) because the pages' own load functions null their state and reset filters. Wired into Dashboard, Inventory, Alerts and Activity. Deliberately NOT Action Items or Table: they fetch a projection per at-risk SKU, so a poll would multiply server calls.

  Verified in a real browser: Dashboard hero went from SGD 1.99M to 1.93M after a pick made outside the page, no reload. The automation tab reports `visibilityState: hidden`, so the hook correctly did nothing until the flag was overridden on that page for the test; a real foreground tab is not affected. `npx vite build` passes.

  **Finding worth knowing for the demo:** Demo mode is cookie-routed. A browser with the `stocksense_demo` cookie reads and writes a separate sandbox database (it showed 5 SKUs, about SGD 1.99M) while a browser without it uses the main database (10 SKUs, about SGD 3.71M). So a handheld and a desktop only reflect each other when BOTH are in the same mode. Curl tests hit the main DB and never touched what the demo-mode browser showed, which cost some confusion here. `npm run seed` resets the main DB only, not the sandbox.

- **Follow-up, same day:** Stan asked to gate the auto-refresh to demo mode, since polling on the real deployment costs server load for no benefit. `useLiveRefresh` now asks `getDemoStatus()` once on mount and only starts polling if active (a demo enter/exit does a full reload, so the answer cannot go stale). Outside demo mode the Control Tower pages behave exactly as before. Consequence: on the main database a desktop still needs a reload to see handheld movements. Build passes; the gated path was not re-run in the browser.

- **Follow-up 2, same day: making a phone and a desktop land in the same demo database.** Files: frontend/src/components/DemoModeBadge.jsx, App.jsx, warehouse/Handheld.jsx, index.css. The sandbox is one shared in-memory database, but each browser opts in with its own cookie, and the handheld screens showed no sign of which database they were on. Two changes. (1) `DemoModeBadge` takes `compact` and `bannerOnly` and is mounted on Goods In and Goods Out: a short "DEMO MODE. Sandbox data" banner while in demo mode, nothing otherwise (no floating Enter button over the handheld header), so a phone and desktop that disagree are visible at a glance. `.hh-screen` min-height and the sticky header now allow for `--demo-banner-height`. (2) A join link: any page carrying the badge honours `?demo=1`, which enters demo mode for THAT browser and strips the param, so opening `/warehouse/inbound?demo=1` on the phone (or scanning it as a QR code) puts it in the same sandbox as the desktop. The param is stripped so a reload after Exit does not silently re-enter.

  Verified in a real browser using `[::1]` as a second device (a separate cookie jar from `localhost`): no banner before the link; after it, banner shown, param stripped, and `/api/warehouse/outbound` returned the sandbox's 5 orders and SGD 1,928,300, not the main database's 13 orders. Not tested: a real phone, and the banner on Goods In (same component, same mount). Known limits left alone: the sandbox is in memory, so a server restart wipes it, and anyone pressing Exit ends it for every device. Also noted: the Vite dev server listens on IPv6 loopback only, so a phone on the LAN cannot reach it in development; the deployed container is unaffected.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (change prompts in live and demo mode, and keeping public judges off the live database)
- **Branch:** feature/goods-out
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/inventory.js (new `GET /api/data-version`), backend/src/routes/warehouse.js, frontend/src/lib/dataVersion.js (new), hooks/useLiveRefresh.js (rewritten), components/NewDataPill.jsx (new), components/DemoModeBadge.jsx, App.jsx, api/inventory.js, warehouse/Handheld.jsx, Inbound.jsx, Outbound.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan asked for a "new data, refresh" prompt for the live database and the same for demo mode so nobody refreshes by hand, and for the earlier recommendation on the public-server risk to be followed.

  **1. Change detection.** `/api/data-version` returns a fingerprint from six cheap lookups (max ids of audit_log, goods_movements, decisions, alerts_log, latest inventory_positions.last_updated, position count). It replaces the timer-driven full refetch: `lib/dataVersion.js` is one shared poller (15s, visible tabs only, one request however many subscribers). LIVE mode: `NewDataPill` (mounted in the Control Tower layout) shows "New stock movements. Refresh" and reloads on click; nothing changes under the reader. DEMO mode: `useLiveRefresh` re-fetches silently when the fingerprint moves and the pill flashes "Updated with new stock movements" for 4s. This supersedes the earlier timer-based demo-only polling, and demo mode is now cheaper too (a tiny check, and the full refetch only when something changed). The pill's baseline resets on route change so it never offers to refresh a page that just loaded.

  **2. Public-server guard.** On the deployed server the handheld PINs are printed on the login screen, so any judge could receive or pick against the one real database everyone shares. In `NODE_ENV=production`, `POST /warehouse/inbound/receive` and `/outbound/pick` now refuse outside the demo sandbox (403, `needs_demo: true`); reads and dev machines are unaffected. `ALLOW_LIVE_WAREHOUSE_WRITES=1` lifts it for Stan's own recording on live data (NOT in the deploy doc yet; add it there if Stan wants to record on live data). The handheld shows a "Join the demo and continue" button, which goes to `?demo=1&sample=1`: enters the sandbox and, only if the sandbox has no SKUs, loads the sample catalog so the phone has orders to work against. Plain `?demo=1` never seeds, so a presenter's empty-portfolio onboarding story is not spoilt.

  Verified: live pill in a real browser (SGD 3.71M unchanged when the pick landed elsewhere, pill appeared, click reloaded to 3.69M and cleared); demo auto-update (1.95M to 1.92M with the "Updated" note); the guard against a throwaway production-mode server on port 4010 with a scratch DATA_DIR (refused without the cookie, accepted with it, escape hatch let it through; reads open; real DB untouched; scratch dir removed); the sample join into an empty sandbox (2 POs, 7 orders, params stripped). The automation tab reports `visibilityState: hidden`, so the visibility flag was overridden on the page for the browser checks. Not tested: the JoinDemo button itself in a browser (the dev server is not production, so the 403 never fires there), and a real phone.
  
  **Watch out:** the in-memory sandbox is wiped by any backend restart, including the dev server reloading on a backend file edit. That happened three times this session and is the same fragility noted earlier. `npm run seed` still resets only the main database.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (Market signals: news to stock impact, replay first)
- **Branch:** feature/market-signals (cut from feature/goods-out; nothing pushed)
- **Files changed:** backend/src/engines/signals.js (new), engines/riskbuffer.js, routes/signals.js (new), routes/inventory.js untouched, index.js, db/init.js (table `market_signals`, variety columns on `risk_events`), db/replayEvents.js (new), db/seed.js, db/audit.js; frontend components/MarketSignals.jsx (new), pages/ActionItems.jsx, pages/Activity.jsx, api/inventory.js, index.css; backend/scripts/test-signals.js (new); design.md, requirements.md (REQ-25), handoff.md
- **Notes:** Stan asked for an agent that watches news affecting supply speed or SKU availability and suggests front-loading or ordering early. Researched what is deployed first (Resilinc, Everstream, Interos, Kinaxis/o9/Blue Yonder): they alert on events and map them to supplier networks; none of what I could read ties an event to one SKU's cover and reorder maths. Stan's calls: rules then model for reading headlines, replay first with the live feed second, surfaced in Action Items.

  Built the replay half. `engines/signals.js` is deterministic and never calls a model: a signal (country or supplier, event type, severity, direction, variety scope) matches SKUs, a fixed table gives `[low, high]` days, and the existing projection engine gives stock-out day, empty days, latest order date and order quantity at both ends of the range. Approving adds a `risk_events` row (so the existing reorder point reflects it) and is audited as SIGNAL_DECIDED; withdrawing switches it off. Nothing creates an order. Six real past events are hand-classified in `db/replayEvents.js`, with dates and measures checked against sources on 19 Sep (India's 8 Sep 2022 broken rice ban, 20 Jul 2023 non-basmati ban, 27 Aug 2023 basmati US$1,200 floor price, the 28 Sep 2024 lifting, and Thai and Vietnamese spillover).

  Two design fixes came from looking at real output, not from the plan. (1) India's non-basmati bans flagged nothing for the basmati SKUs, correctly, but a plain text match would have got "Non-Basmati" wrong; `isVariety` uses a lookbehind, tested. (2) TJ-25KG has 29 days of cover against a 45 day lead time, so it was already short before any news and the tool blamed the headline; the scenario now separates `days_without_stock_without_signal` from `days_added_by_signal`. A third came from clicking Approve: `riskbuffer.js` caps the total at 30 days and the seeded India event already contributes 21, so "Add a 15.5 day buffer" really added 9. The screen now shows each product's buffer before and after, with "capped".

  Verified: `test-signals.js` 23 checks with hand-worked figures, proven able to fail by three deliberate breaks (window ignoring the delay, non-basmati matching as basmati, a zeroed playbook row); `check-formulas.js` still 19 of 19 after the `riskbuffer.js` change; a real browser walk (replay, load, approve, buffer 21 to 30 and reorder point with risk 45.9 to 50.9); API paths (approving an easing event refused, double approve 409, withdraw restores 21, replay idempotent, unknown fixture 404). Main database reseeded afterwards. Not tested: the layout at true desktop width (the automation window was narrow, so only the stacked layout was seen), a mobile pass, and Activity's new SIGNAL_DECIDED row in the browser.

  **Open for Stan:** the seeded "India non-basmati export restriction" is unscoped and buffers the India basmati SKUs by 21 days although basmati was exempt; scoping it would drop those buffers to 0 and change displayed reorder points, so it was left. **Not built:** the live feed (Google News RSS search returned 200 with 37 fresh items in one second; GDELT answered 429 asking for one request per five seconds, twice) and the rules-then-model headline reader. The Google News route is unofficial and its terms were not checked. Any model call to read headlines needs Stan's approval for paid runs; local llama3.1:8b would be the free path.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (Market signals part 2: live news scan and headline reader)
- **Branch:** feature/market-signals (nothing pushed)
- **Files changed:** backend/src/signals/feed.js, signals/reader.js, middleware/sandboxGuard.js (new; the handheld's guard moved here), routes/signals.js (scan, correct, merge), routes/warehouse.js, db/init.js (`signal_seen`, `also_reported_by`), db/seed.js; scripts/test-signal-reader.js, scripts/bench-signal-reader.js (new); frontend MarketSignals.jsx, api/inventory.js, Activity.jsx; design.md, requirements.md, handoff.md
- **Notes:** Built the live half Stan approved: rules first, then a model, then a strict check. Feed is Google News RSS (tested: 198 headlines in one scan, no key); the reader is a keyword filter, then local `llama3.1:8b`, then a validator that accepts only fixed-list values and only origins the portfolio buys from; keyword fallback when there is no model or the answer fails. Never the paid tier: `provider.chat()` silently falls back to the server default when local is unavailable, and that could be paid, so the reader checks `resolveTier("local")` first (tested, and proven able to fail by three deliberate breaks).

  **Measured, not assumed.** The bench (14 labelled headlines, 3 passes) gave 76 percent at baseline; the model rated nearly everything "high", including India lifting a ban. A severity rubric fixed that (accuracy 71 to 79 percent across runs, noisy). Adding worked examples to the prompt fixed the China case but made the model OBEY the hijack headline 3 of 3 times and dropped the India headlines, so it was reverted. Stopped tuning there: an 8B model has a ceiling and chasing single headlines overfits. One of my own labels (Japan retail prices easing) was a judgement call and was changed to "not relevant", stated in the file.

  **Real news exposed real problems the plan did not have.** Four to seven outlets covering India's September drought became four to seven cards: now merged into one with an "also reported by" list. The read cap of 8 left 154 of 198 headlines waiting: raised to 16 (about 20 seconds). And since a model right three times in four cannot be the last word, every reading is labelled with who made it and a person can correct the four fields (assessment recomputes). Scan, replay, accept, dismiss and correct are sandbox-only on a public server, via the shared guard.

  Verified: 27 reader checks, 23 engine checks, formula check 19 of 19; a real scan through the API and through the browser (151 headlines, 3 new, 10 merged, "Read by AI (llama3.1:8b, on this machine)"); cooldown 429; corrections refuse an unknown country and a smuggled "add 90 days"; a correction in the browser recomputed the assessment (7 to 14 became 14 to 28 days). **Not tested:** the scan on the deployed server (no Ollama there, so it is the keyword list alone, coarser: it cannot tell a buyer restricting exporters from a supplier banning exports, so it never rates above medium); Google's terms for automated access; a scheduled background scan (a person presses the button); the merge and cooldown logic have no automated test, only the real runs above. Sandbox and main database both hold scan results from testing; `npm run seed` clears the main one only.
