The browser window would not resize, hence the iframes. Reproduced the reported bug and found its causes, then fixed and re-ran until the audit was clean.

  **The overlaps, three causes.** (1) The mobile top bar is `position: fixed; top: 0` and ignored the banner, so the banner sat on top of it: now `top: var(--demo-banner-height)`. (2) The banner had a fixed 40px height and a long sentence that wrapped below ~900px and overflowed: its height is now measured (`ResizeObserver`), the text is short on small screens, and it is one line. (3) Out of demo mode a fixed "Enter demo mode" pill covered **Bulk edit and Add SKU** on desktop and the phone nav: removed; entry moved to Settings and to Home (`lib/demoMode.js`). Also: the handheld screens now clear the banner (min-height and sticky header offset), the refresh pill reserves room so it cannot hide a page's last line, and a Dashboard nudge card that squeezed its text into a 100px column on phones now wraps.

  **Contrast, app-wide, decided by me and flagged for Stan.** White on `--blue` was 3.7:1 and accent colours as text were 2.1 to 4.2:1. Light theme accents shifted one step darker in `:root` (`--blue` #3b82f6 to #2563eb, `--red` #ef4444 to #c62828, `--purple` #8b5cf6 to #7c3aed, `--text-muted` #64748b to #5a6a80) and text-safe `*-text` tokens added (yellow, green and orange cannot be darkened globally without turning chart fills brown). The dark theme restores bright accents and lightens red and purple. One-line revert per token if Stan dislikes the look. About 75 text colour uses and a handful of white-text buttons were repointed.

  **Touch and other.** 44px targets on phones via global rules and `.hit-44`, `.hit-44-icon`, `.touch-44` (phone nav tabs now 44 tall with the Home label reduced to an icon); 28px minimum with a mouse. Every select and icon control named. `<summary>` gets a focus ring. `&mdash;` entities and dash placeholders removed from visible copy on several pages (rule: no dashes). One primary per surface: "Scan the news now" is quiet while a signal is pending.

  **Mistakes worth recording.** I appended a 28px desktop minimum after the 44px phone rule at equal specificity, silently cancelling it; scoped to `min-width: 769px`. A `touch-44` on the health bar clipped it inside a fixed-height container; the container now grows too. Two audit false alarms came from the hidden automation tab freezing CSS transitions, so a colour inheriting from `body` looked wrong; the harness now disables transitions.

  **Verified (final):** all 12 routes clean at all 4 widths, in live and demo mode, light theme; dark theme clean on 8 routes at 2 widths. **Known exceptions, deliberate:** a disabled "Back" on Onboarding (WCAG exempts disabled controls); chart axis and value labels at 10 to 11px (the type scale's own carve-out). **Not covered:** dark theme at 768 and 1024, Goods In steps 2 to 4 visually, Alerts and Activity by screenshot (audit only), a real phone, and true keyboard focus order. The temporary harness (`public/__audit.js`) was deleted and was never committed.

## Session: 2026-09-19 (the UI/UX audit becomes a reusable skill, and the untested areas get tested)
- **Branch:** feature/market-signals (nothing pushed)
- **Files changed:** `.claude/skills/ui-ux-audit/` (new: SKILL.md, checklist.md, pitfalls.md, scripts/ux-audit.js, run-audit.mjs, selftest.mjs, examples/), `.kiro/steering/ui-ux-audit.md` (new pointer), `frontend/ux-audit.config.json` (new), package.json (`playwright-core` devDependency, `ux:audit` and `ux:selftest` scripts), package-lock.json (new), .gitignore, docs/DIRECTORY.md, handoff.md, rules.md; app fixes in Dashboard, ForecastList, Home, Onboarding, CatalogFields, MarketSignals, index.css
- **Notes:** Stan asked to test what the last pass did not cover, and to save the UI/UX testing approach as a skill for Kiro or Claude. The earlier pass was limited by an extension-driven browser that would not resize, so it used same-origin iframes and could not press real keys or emulate touch. The skill fixes that: a headless runner on `playwright-core` driving the installed Chrome (real viewports, touch emulation, real Tab key), and the earlier in-page detector kept as a second mode.

  **The skill, generic on purpose.** One canonical copy in `.claude/skills/ui-ux-audit/` (Claude Code finds it there); Kiro gets a small `inclusion: manual` steering file pointing at the same folder, so nothing is duplicated. `ux-audit.js` reads geometry and computed style only, so it knows no framework: 15 checks with stable ids (overlap, clipping, overflow, type size, contrast, forbidden text, target size, accessible names, broken images, page basics, and keyboard focus visibility, coverage and traps). Everything app-specific lives in a per-project JSON config (routes, scripted multi-step states, themes, modes, thresholds, and an `ignore` list where every entry needs a reason and stays visible in the report). `SKILL.md` is the procedure (checklist first, measure, triage, root-cause fix, re-run everything, look at screenshots, record exceptions, report what was not covered); `pitfalls.md` catalogues the root causes found this session so the next project starts from them.

  **A self-test proved the checks can fail, and immediately caught five silent failures.** `selftest.mjs` loads a deliberately broken page and its clean twin. First run: five checks did not fire. Two were fixture mistakes; one was real browser behaviour worth recording (a phone page with no viewport tag widens its layout instead of overflowing, so horizontal overflow is invisible until the tag exists; now reported as X2 with that explanation).

  **What testing the uncovered areas found (268 audits: 25 states x live/demo x light/dark x 1440/1024/768/390).** Dark theme at all widths, every Goods In and Goods Out step, Alerts, Activity and Market signals with data were clean. It found, in the app: the Dashboard health-bar buttons and the Forecast sort headers had no keyboard focus ring (an inline `outline: none` and an inline `all: unset` beat the global rule); Home's "Receiving" label was 2.28:1 green text; a summary toggle was 23px tall; raw 10px, 12px and 16px font sizes on Onboarding, the catalog fields page and the handheld buttons broke the type-scale rule. All fixed. In the tool: "focus stuck" on Activity and Forecast was a false alarm (rows with identical labels looked like one control), fixed by keying focus on position too; and the refresh-pill state failed on a second run because it consumes purchase orders, so failed API calls now fail loudly with the HTTP status instead of a mystery timeout, and the state's reliance on a fresh `npm run seed` is documented.

  **Verified:** full matrix, then the touched states re-run (140 audits): 0 errors, 0 warnings; real-keyboard Tab test clean on every route at 1440 and 390. Recorded exceptions (visible in every report): chart axis labels at 10 to 11px, and the refresh pill covering content by design. **Not covered:** a real phone, screen readers, 200 percent zoom, live window resizing, and the visual judgement the audit cannot make (hierarchy, alignment, copy). The skill was created mid-session, so Claude Code will list it from the next session onward. `playwright-core` is a new devDependency (about 3 MB, downloads no browser); Stan should know it was added.

- **Notes:** Step 7 of Stan's Reorder Loop, "Separate the duties": two roles, two permissions on the same inventory record, enforced server-side rather than assumed. Stan approved the two open design calls in chat: (A) remove the office restock rather than relocate it, and (B) no manager login for the demo — enforce only the write boundary and the warehouse roles, not a Control Tower identity. The PDF build map's own header says it is not a build spec until Stan signs off, so both were confirmed before any code.
  Three parts. First, enforcement of a column that already existed but was never checked: `operators.role` (receiving | dispatch | both) was read by /warehouse/login and returned, then ignored. A new `requireOperatorRole(db, res, operatorId, action)` in warehouse.js now gates the two stock-writing endpoints — a RECEIPT needs receiving|both, an ISSUE needs dispatch|both — returning 401 when nobody is signed in and 403 with a named reason ("Taw is not cleared to receive stock. Ask a receiving operator to confirm this.") when the role is wrong. It replaces the plain "operator exists and is active" check both endpoints used, so it is one guard with two callers, not a second rule to keep in sync.
  Second, closing the one endpoint that contradicted the rule. `POST /api/inventory/restock` lived on the Control Tower router and added on_hand_qty straight from the office, with no purchase order, no operator and no variance — exactly the "Control Tower changes stock" the step forbids. Removed, with a comment block left in its place so the next reader learns why the office has no stock write rather than wondering where it went. New `order_requests` table (request_no REQ-000N, sku_id, quantity_mt, reason, status open|ordered|cancelled, requested_by), added to seed.js's wipe list and sequence reset ahead of skus for the FK. New `POST /api/order-requests` (validates a positive quantity and a known SKU, writes the row, logs a new ORDER_REQUESTED audit event) and `GET /api/order-requests`. The audit event deliberately carries no output stock figures, because the whole point of the event is that the physical position did not move. Inventory.jsx's old Restock button became "Request order", prefilled with the SKU's suggested_order_qty, opening a modal that sends to the buyer and confirms in words that stock will not change until the delivery is received. Activity.jsx gained an ORDER_REQUESTED line (Send icon, "Order requested"); the retired RESTOCK renderer stays so any older audit rows still read, the same reason LLM_MODE_CHANGED's renderer was kept.

## Session: 2026-09-17 (Reorder Loop step 7 — follow-on: consume the request + seed it)
- **Branch:** feature/mvp2-separate-duties (continues the entry above)

- **Notes:** Two additions Stan asked for after the core step, to stop an order request from sitting open forever and to make the feature visible on a fresh demo. Neither changes stock; the step's whole invariant still holds.
  First, consuming the request. New `PATCH /api/order-requests/:id { status }` moves an open request to 'ordered' (a real order now exists for the warehouse to receive against) or 'cancelled' (dropped). It is a one-way step from 'open', the same shape alert acknowledgement uses, so a stale tab cannot flip an already-decided line back: a second transition returns 409. Validation returns 400 for a status other than ordered|cancelled and 404 for an unknown id. A new ORDER_REQUEST_UPDATED audit event records it, again with no output stock figures, because the point of step 7 is that this office action moves nothing physical. The frontend gained `api.updateOrderRequest(id, status)` and an "Open buyer requests" card on the Inventory page, above the table and hidden entirely when nothing is open, listing each open request (quantity, SKU, request number, reason) with "Mark ordered" and "Cancel". Acting on one reloads only the request list, not the SKU rows, since no stock changed; a failed transition (someone else closed it first) silently refreshes the list rather than showing an error box.
  Second, seeding. Two illustrative order_requests in seed.js, keyed to real SKUs: REQ-0001 (VF-10KG, 200 MT, open) so a demo has a live request to act on, and REQ-0002 (PH-25KG, 150 MT, ordered) so the lifecycle past 'open' shows without a click first. The seed hardcodes REQ-0001/0002 and nextRequestNo derives from the highest existing row, so the next runtime request is REQ-0003 with no collision (checked). The seed console summary now reports the order-request count alongside the others.
  Verified live: reseed reports "2 order requests"; the open filter returns only REQ-0001 (the ordered one correctly excluded); PATCH open->ordered succeeds and leaves VF-10KG on-hand at 620 before and after; a repeat transition is 409, a bad status 400, an unknown id 404; the ORDER_REQUEST_UPDATED audit row is written. check-formulas.js 23/23 clean, vite build passes. Reseeded afterward to clear the test transition. No paid calls. Not committed.
  Still open, unchanged: order_requests has no link to a real purchase order (marking 'ordered' records intent, it does not create a PO the inbound flow can receive against), and there is still no manager identity on the office write (requested_by defaults to "control tower"). Both are deliberate scope lines, noted here so the next person does not read the loop as fully closed.

## Session: 2026-09-20 (order requests: a timeline, the minimal version)
- **Branch:** feature/order-request-timeline
- **Files changed:** backend/src/db/init.js, backend/src/db/seed.js, backend/src/routes/inventory.js, backend/src/routes/demo.js, frontend/src/api/inventory.js, frontend/src/components/OrderRequestsCard.jsx (new), frontend/src/pages/Inventory.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Activity.jsx, requirements.md, the submission tracker
- **Notes:** Stan asked for the office's request to the buyer to show where it has got to, and asked me to vet the idea against industry practice first. The vetting changed two things: "buyer read the message" became an explicit "acknowledged" step (ERPs use actions, not read receipts, and there is no buyer login to detect a read), and rejection and cancellation were added, because an approval that can only say yes is not one. Steps: open, acknowledged (buyer), po_raised (buyer), approved or rejected (buyer's manager, a reason is required to reject), cancelled from any earlier step. Anything else is 409, a bad status 400. The actor is fixed by the step on the server, so a client cannot claim another role. Each step is a row in the new append-only `order_request_events` table, written in one transaction with the status change, and also an ORDER_REQUEST_UPDATED audit event. The old 'ordered' status became 'approved' (a one-line migration in init.js, idempotent).
  The card moved out of Inventory.jsx into its own component and was renamed "Requests waiting for the buyer" (Stan's wording). It shows each active request with its state, one primary button for the next step, Reject (inline reason) and Cancel, and an expandable timeline with date and time. It says plainly that one person plays every role in the demo. ORDER_REQUEST_UPDATED had no Activity renderer before; it now reads as a sentence and has a filter chip. Seed: REQ-0001 is open with one step, REQ-0002 is approved with a full timeline.
  A bug caught on the way: the sample-data trim (`trimSampleCatalog`) must delete events before requests, because events point at requests; added and checked (no orphans). Verified: API walk of every allowed and refused step (409, 400, 404), stock unchanged at 620 throughout; vite build; the whole flow in a real browser in light theme (acknowledge, raise, reject with reason, then Activity); reseeded afterwards. The legacy 'ordered' migration is one trivial UPDATE and was not run against an old database. No paid calls.
  Not built, on purpose: the request never becomes a real purchase order, so it does not close itself when Goods In receives the delivery, and the card drops a request once it ends. The steps to do that, in order, are in the submission tracker, "Order requests: what is built and how to do the fuller version".

- **Notes:** Stan asked for the look-back to be a control, with presets and a custom option with a limit. Now a "Look back" dropdown (Last 3 days, Last week, Last 2 weeks, Last month, Custom) and, for Custom, a whole number of days. I chose presets over a slider or free number on purpose: nobody needs exactly 11 days, and a free number invites 0 or 500. The limits (1 to 30, default 14) live in one place, `SCAN_DAYS` in routes/signals.js, and are sent to the page in the market-signals payload, so the screen and the server cannot disagree. A bad `days` is refused with 400 before the cooldown starts, so it cannot burn a scan. 30 is the ceiling because a scan reads at most 16 headlines: a real 7 day scan fetched 168 headlines, read 14 relevant ones and left 63 for the next scan, so a longer window mostly adds a queue. The page says so when the window is over 2 weeks.
  Behaviour at the default is unchanged: the search asks for `when:Nd` and items older than N+7 days are dropped, which is exactly the old 14 day search with a 21 day filter. Two new checks in test-signal-reader.js (29 pass), each proven able to fail by breaking the code they cover. The route was tried with 0, 31, 2.5, "abc" and -3 (all 400). Verified in a real browser: the custom field turns red and Scan disables when out of range; a valid 7 day scan ran and the result note said "from the last 7 days" in 26 seconds. The docs had said "21 day window" (design.md) and the card "last 3 weeks"; the query always asked for 14 days, both corrected. No paid calls; the scan uses the local model or a wordlist and cannot reach the paid tier.

He was right: the server sorts every signal into one list (pending first, then newest), so a rehearsal of a 2023 event could sit above real news. The Run function itself was fine (the event loads, the picker's catalog shrinks, running one twice is harmless). The fix is in the screen: the Live news | Past events control now decides which signals are listed below it, with its own heading ("Live news signals", "Past events you have run"), its own empty message, and a blue count on each tab for signals still waiting for a decision, so nothing hides in the tab you are not looking at. The live scan's result note shows only on Live news. The empty message names how many wait in the other tab.
  The Past events help now says plainly that adding a buffer from a past event changes real reorder points, and that Dismiss is the way to only look. That is how the code behaves today (approving inserts a risk_events row whatever the signal's origin), and it is worth Stan's decision whether a replayed event should be approvable at all or dismiss-only "practice". Not changed here. Verified in a real browser (demo sandbox): a real scan filled Live news with 5 and a badge, Past events showed none until one was run, then showed only that event, with both badges right. Reseeded the real database afterwards. Frontend only. No paid calls.
## Session: 2026-09-20 (Market signals: grouped, folded, with undo)
- **Branch:** feature/market-signals-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/routes/signals.js, frontend/src/components/MarketSignals.jsx, frontend/src/pages/Activity.jsx, design.md
- **Notes:** Stan found the Live news list overwhelming when there are many signals. Each signal was a full card of 300 to 500px, and the least useful ones (no effect on the stock) took as much room as the urgent ones. Now the list is grouped by what the reader has to do, using the same test the card already used to offer "Add buffer": Needs a decision (worst urgency first, always open), No effect on your stock (folded by default, Stan's call), and Decided (folded, with a count of active buffers). Each signal is a one line row (date, country, event type, headline, and an urgency pill with the number of products, or why nothing changes) that opens into the full card, one at a time; the first needing a decision opens itself, and after a decision the next one does. A summary line states the counts. "Acknowledge all" sits beside the No effect title and applies to that group only (Stan's call), never to anything needing a decision.
  Undo, as Stan asked: a new server decision, `reopen`, allowed only from dismissed (409 otherwise; an approved signal is taken back by withdrawing, which is its own step), audited, with an Activity sentence. The screen uses it twice: a 12 second "Acknowledged N signals. Undo" bar after Acknowledge all, and a permanent Reopen button on any dismissed signal in the Decided group. A signal just added by a scan or a replay opens its own group and card, otherwise "Run this event" on an event with no effect would look like it did nothing. The scan buttons lead only when nothing needs a decision.
  Verified: the route by hand (reopen from pending 409, dismiss then reopen 200, reopen again 409, unknown decision 400; there is no automated route test for it, only the manual one); in a real browser (demo sandbox) a scan of 5 signals showed 1 needing a decision and 4 folded; Acknowledge all moved 4 into Decided with the undo bar, Undo restored all 4, Reopen from Decided returned one, and running a no-effect past event opened its card. Real database reseeded afterwards. One thing seen and left alone: the same story can arrive twice when the reader classifies it differently (once "eases", once "no products affected"), because the twin check needs the same event type and direction. No paid calls.
## Session: 2026-09-20 (Market signals: a smarter same-story check)
- **Branch:** feature/market-signals-ux
- **Files changed:** backend/src/signals/twins.js (new), backend/src/routes/signals.js, backend/scripts/test-signal-reader.js, design.md
- **Notes:** Stan asked to make the duplicate check smarter after the same story showed twice ("India eyes new rice export markets..." once read as eases, once as no products affected). Cause: the old test needed the reader's reading to match (same country, event type and direction), and the reader is not consistent; it also skipped dismissed signals, so a syndicated copy could bring a dismissed story back as a new question. Now the text is compared first (`signals/twins.js`): word overlap of 0.6 or more when the country agrees, 0.9 or more whatever the country, within 7 days, against the signal's headline and every wording already merged into it, in any state including dismissed. The old reading test stays as the second check.
  A text score alone is dangerous here, measured not guessed: "India bans non-basmati exports" and "India lifts ban on non-basmati exports" score 0.63, so a lifting would be hidden behind a ban. Two guards sit on top: opposite direction words (judged by the first one each headline uses; my first version, "words from one side only", failed its own test because "lifts ban" contains "ban") and figures that must overlap (10% is not 20%). Both err toward not merging, since a missed merge costs a card and a wrong one hides news. Measured on 241 real headlines: 23 pairs merge (with the country assumed to agree, the most generous case), all genuine repeats except one pair of list articles about two countries that the country rule keeps apart in real use; 0.5 to 0.58 pairs (mostly the Pakistan and Saudi Arabia story) stay apart on their own and join through the other wordings once one is attached.
  Nine new checks (38 pass), each guard proven able to fail by disabling it in turn. A real scan, three passes to drain the queue, ended with 8 signals and the story above once, with one other source attached, and none repeated. Real database reseeded afterwards. No paid calls (the scan cannot reach the paid tier).

Vetted first: industry keeps a to-do list separate from the audit record and joins them at the item (PagerDuty, CloudWatch and CloudTrail, Jira's history tab), and the Market signals list had already shown that mixing two kinds of thing in one list misleads. So the tab is merged and the lists are not: `/alerts` has two views, Needs action and History, chosen in the URL (`?view=history`), sharing a product filter (`?sku=`); `/activity` redirects; the sidebar loses its Activity item. Added `POST /api/alerts/:id/reopen` (only from acknowledged, else 409; audited as ALERT_REOPENED), `GET /api/alerts/handled`, `GET /api/alerts/:id/history`; the audit endpoint takes several event types and counts scoped to a product. Dismiss now has a 12 second undo bar and errors are shown instead of only logged.
  Refinements: Needs action groups by severity with a plain empty state that names what to do next; refresh no longer blanks the page; the modals fit a phone (they were a fixed 480 and 560px), have dialog roles and named close buttons, and the emoji glyphs are icons. History: 16 type chips became 5 categories, day headings with exact times, "Show older events" paging, natural sentences ("Dismissed the Idle Stock alert on ..."), and Reopen only on the latest dismissal of an alert. Verified in a real browser: dismiss and undo (9, 8, 9), History rows and chips, reopen with its notice, Open alert with the highlight ring, the product filter across both views, /activity redirect. API tried by hand for every path (409, 404, counts). No automated route test for the new endpoints, only that manual one.

## Session: 2026-09-20 (model layer made ready for Sonnet, without spending)
- **Branch:** fix/llm-sonnet-readiness (uncommitted when written)
- **Files changed:** backend/src/llm/tone.js, slots.js, provider.js, explain.js, explainActionItem.js, askDatabase.js, backend/scripts/test-llm-sonnet.js (new), sonnet-check.js, frontend/src/lib/modelName.js (new), pages/Alerts.jsx, components/ActivityFeed.jsx, design.md, handoff.md, the tracker
- **Notes:** Stan asked that the LLM features work as smoothly on Sonnet as on llama, with no bugs in the logic. Only the alert Why? has ever run on Sonnet (four paid calls in September); Action Items Why? and Ask about your data never had. Read the whole layer and found gaps that a llama-only history hid: nothing removed markdown, backticks or dashes (the no-dash rule lived only in the prompts); nothing detected an answer cut off at the token cap (280, sized for llama); a doubled unit ("{lead_time} days") was retried, and on the paid tier a retry is a second charge (Ask already fixed it by rule, the other two did not, and it failed a real Action Items answer in a free rehearsal on llama); a paid Ask that failed after billed calls wrote no audit row, so spend.js never saw it; the Why? label and Settings would have shown the gateway's raw id.
  Fixes, all by rule after the model answers, per the project rule: `cleanFormatting`, `looksCutOff` and `tidy` in tone.js applied by all three pipelines; `stripDoubledUnits` moved to slots.js and used by all three, extended to "a day" after a rate; `truncated` reported by provider.js (Ollama `done_reason`, Anthropic `stop_reason`); a separate cloud output cap of 420 (`LLM_CLOUD_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`) so the local cap the benchmark measures is untouched; a failed Ask now logs an LLM_CALL row with provider and model; `friendlyModel` and `modelLabel` show "Claude Sonnet 4.5" to people while the audit keeps the id for pricing.
  Proof without spending: `test-llm-sonnet.js`, 23 checks, fake Ollama and gateway servers plus scripted Sonnet-style answers (wire layer and daily cap, then all three features). Mutation-tested: breaking the clean-up, the cut-off check, the failed-Ask log, the cloud cap and the unit rule each made it fail. The PIN gate was checked on all three routes with no pass and a forged pass (refused, no model call, audit trail shows no paid rows), and in the browser each screen showed its locked message and cleared the bad pass. First benchmark after the changes (llama3, 4 passes, 9 alerts): 97% first-try, 1.03 calls per explanation, 0 dangerous, 0 contradictions, matching the recorded numbers. A second run after the unit-rule change in explain.js gave the same: 97% first-try, 1.03 calls per explanation, 0 dangerous, 0 contradictions, 100% usable, 4.0 s latency.
  `sonnet-check.js --features` now also covers Action Items Why? and two Ask questions through the running server (plan and estimate with no flag, `--dry-run` for a free llama rehearsal). Still unknown until the real paid run, which needs Stan's approval: Sonnet's exact wording against the tone and semantic checks, and whether it keeps to the one-line CALL format in Ask. Found and not changed: the seed has never produced a REORDER alert (checked against the commit before today), so the paid check covers five of the six types; Ask needs a local model, so it is unavailable on the deployed server.
  **Stan then asked for three more fixes and the real Sonnet run** (same day). Fixes: a failed answer now reaches the screen in plain words ("no answer passed our checks, so none is shown. Try naming one product by its code...") through `plainReason` in provider.js for all three routes, with the technical reason kept in the audit trail; an unknown address now shows a "That page does not exist" page (`pages/NotFound.jsx`, a catch-all route) instead of a blank screen; a dash bullet run on after a colon goes on its own line. Pre-flight for the paid run: all local suites green, the same run rehearsed free on llama, PIN fetched by the script from backend/.env and sent only to the local server, never printed.
  **The real run** (once, `sonnet-check.js --confirm-spend --features`): 11 model calls over 9 explanations, USD 0.0452, all in the spend ledger (total 0.0663). Every alert explanation was accepted on the first attempt (134 to 252 output tokens, so the 420 cap was never near), both Action Items explanations worked, and Ask followed the one-line CALL format on both questions (a lookup then an answer). What only the real model showed, and the fixes: (1) `calmTone` collapsed every run of whitespace including line breaks, so "Comparison:" then a blank line then bullets ran together; a bug for every non-stockout answer, now horizontal whitespace only. (2) The OVERSTOCK summary said "SGD $568K locked in excess inventory" when 568K is the value of all 620 MT and the excess is 220 MT: the model had been offered `capital_tied_up`, now withheld for OVERSTOCK (kept for SLOW_MOVING, where it is right). (3) Ask opened with "Looking at the facts, I have comprehensive comparison data... I can now provide a complete answer.", now dropped by `stripSelfCommentary`. (4) "a A class item", now "an A class item". Each has a check in test-llm-sonnet.js built from the shape seen and was mutation-tested (29 checks).
  Left as they are: Sonnet's blank lines between bullets (harmless), "45 days minus 29 days" in one Action Items bullet (the model avoided computing, which is the rule; it just reads awkwardly), and one speculative cause in SLOW_MOVING ("past orders were too large") that no figure supports and no check catches. REORDER was not in the run because the seed has no REORDER alert; it was covered by two real Sonnet calls on 14 and 15 Sep. Benchmark after these changes (llama3, 4 passes, 36 runs): 92% first-try, 1.11 calls per explanation, 0 dangerous, 0 contradictions, 100% usable, against 97% and 1.03 an hour earlier. To see whether the cut-off check caused the dip, 27 real explanations were run with every answer measured: the check fired 0 times in 29 calls and calls per explanation was 1.07, so the dip is run to run noise (3 retries instead of 1 in 36), not the change.

- **Branch:** fix/action-item-timing-claims
- **Files changed:** backend/src/llm/explainActionItem.js, backend/scripts/test-llm-sonnet.js, frontend/src/pages/ActionItems.jsx
- **Notes:** Stan's first live Why? on Action Items (Thai Jasmine 25KG, 28 days of stock, 45 days to deliver) produced a correct opening sentence and a bullet saying an order "arrives after you're already in trouble", then a final bullet saying "Order now so the new stock arrives before you run low". Both cannot be true. Fixed in three layers, in the order rules.md prefers: (1) the engine now writes the delivery gap itself as part of the opening sentence ("An order placed today would arrive about 17 days after it runs out, so the suggested order shortens that gap rather than avoiding it"), computed from lead time minus days to stockout and absent when an order is already covered or delivery is in time (`timingGap`); this also feeds the rule-based sentence shown when no model answers; (2) the prompt tells the model to say nothing about arrival timing when a gap exists; (3) a backstop rule (`dropTimingPromises`) removes any bullet promising new stock arrives before or in time, or that ordering avoids running out, only when a gap exists. The cache key now includes lead time. The answer box also says which model wrote it and that the opening sentence is the engines' own, as the Alerts box does.
  Tests: two new checks in `test-llm-sonnet.js` (31 checks pass in total): the exact contradictory answer gets the gap sentence and loses the false bullets while true bullets stay, and with no gap the same wording is kept and no gap sentence is added. Proved able to fail: with the backstop rule switched off the first check fails on the contradictory bullet. Real local-model run on TJ-25KG shows the gap sentence. `test-tone.js` 19 pass, `ux:audit --only action-items` 0 errors. `bench-models.js` was not re-run: it measures the alert Why? pipeline, which this change does not touch.

## Session: 2026-09-21 (deploy guide brought in line with what happened)
- **Branch:** docs/deploy-guide-learnings

- **Notes:** Stan asked for the deploy steps written up. Rather than a second document, the existing deploy guide was corrected and extended: the redeploy path that worked twice (Modify keeps the PIN and key, so only the image tag changes; the env values are visible on that form and must not be screenshotted; a redeploy resets the container's counter and database, so paid calls go in the ledger by hand), what the first deploy actually looked like (the service is `container-service-1` and cannot be renamed, a quota message on the first Create, Create and deploy are two steps, only the package needs to be public), and the by-hand check now uses the inline PIN field on Action Items. A private phone copy was published as an artifact. Screenshots were left out on purpose because the deployment form shows the PIN and key in plain text, and the browser tool cannot save them to files.
